- **New SFX_SHUTTER** — a procedural camera shutter sound (short 0.04s noise sweep at 0.15 volume) mimicking a mechanical camera click — the universal audio shorthand for "photo taken". Generated via `_gen_noise_sweep(0.04, 0.15)`.
- **Shutter SFX on screenshot capture** — pressing F to capture a screenshot in photo mode now plays `SFX_SHUTTER` alongside the existing white flash overlay. Previously the screenshot capture had a visual flash but no audio — the camera shutter sound completes the "taking a photo" feedback loop.
- **Photo mode enter SFX** — entering photo mode (F9) now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` to confirm the mode transition. Previously entering photo mode had only a HUD message.
- **Photo mode exit SFX** — exiting photo mode now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch (slightly lower, concluding) to mirror the enter sound. Previously exiting had only a HUD message.

## Enhancement Pack 14 — Endgame & Dungeon Event Feedback Polish

### Dungeon Boss Death Juice (`dungeon_generator.gd`)
- **Camera trauma + particle burst on dungeon boss death** — `_on_dungeon_boss_died()` now triggers 0.4 camera trauma + a sparkle burst in the dungeon theme color at the boss's position. Previously the dungeon boss kill had `SFX_BOSS_DEFEATED` + a text message but no physical feedback — arena bosses, world bosses, and mode-driven bosses all got camera trauma on death, but dungeon bosses were left out. The 0.4 trauma matches the mode-driven boss spawn weight (Boss Rush/Gauntlet bosses get 0.4-0.5 on spawn, so the death should match). The theme-colored sparkle (stone/crystal/volcanic/void/digital) gives the celebration a dungeon-specific visual identity.

### Ancient Vault Puzzle Completion Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **Camera trauma on vault guardian awakening** — `_complete_vault_puzzle()` now triggers 0.5 camera trauma alongside the existing `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN`. Previously the moment the 800-HP Vault Guardian erupted from the solved puzzle had audio but no physical feedback — one of the most dramatic events in the endgame was felt only through sound. The 0.5 trauma matches boss-gauntlet-completion weight, conveying the gravity of an ancient guardian awakening.

### Ancient Vault Guardian Death Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **Camera trauma + fireworks on vault guardian death** — `_on_vault_guardian_died()` now triggers 0.5 camera trauma + a 3-color combo fireworks burst at the vault position. Previously clearing the Ancient Vault — one of the hardest endgame achievements (10 lore stones + Simon-says puzzle + 800-HP Drake boss) — had `SFX_BOSS_DEFEATED` + `SFX_CHEST_OPEN` + a text message but no physical or visual celebration. The 3-color fireworks (matching the loot-cave-clear celebration but with an extra color) gives the ultimate endgame victory a multi-sensory crescendo matching its difficulty.

### Loot Cave Interior Entry Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **SFX + camera trauma on loot cave entry** — `enter_loot_cave()` now plays `SFX_FAST_TRAVEL` + triggers 0.25 camera trauma when the player is teleported into the cave interior. Previously the loot cave *discovery* moment had `SFX_CHEST_OPEN` + 0.2 trauma, but the actual *descent* into the cave (the teleport 6m underground) was completely silent — the player went from surface to cave interior with no sensory feedback, making the transition feel like a menu action rather than a physical descent. The `SFX_FAST_TRAVEL` + 0.25 trauma matches the dungeon enter feedback (`SFX_FAST_TRAVEL` + 0.3 trauma) for a consistent "entering a dangerous place" audio-visual language across both underground content types.

### NG Tier Change Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **SFX + camera trauma + HUD message on NG tier cycle** — `set_ng_tier()` now plays SFX + triggers camera trauma + shows an HUD message when the player cycles NG tiers (Shift+N). Normal tier uses `SFX_UI_CLICK` + 0.1 trauma + "NG tier: Normal" message; NG+/NG++ use `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN` + 0.3 trauma + "🔥 NG tier set to: [name]!" message. Previously the tier change was a silent `print()` to the developer console + a signal — the player had no in-game indication that the difficulty tier actually changed when they pressed Shift+N. The heavier SFX + trauma for NG+/NG++ conveys the increased danger.

### NG+ and NG++ Unlock Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **SFX + camera trauma on NG+ unlock** — `_check_unlocks()` now plays `SFX_LEVEL_UP` + triggers 0.35 camera trauma when NG+ unlocks at level 25. Previously the biggest difficulty unlock had only a text message — the player reached level 25 and saw "🔥 NG+ UNLOCKED!" with no sensory feedback. Also persists the unlock immediately via `_save_unlocks()` so the unlock isn't lost if the game crashes before the next periodic save.
- **SFX + camera trauma on NG++ unlock** — NG++ unlock at level 40 gets even heavier feedback: `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN` + `SFX_LEVEL_UP` (dual SFX) + 0.5 camera trauma. NG++ is the hardest tier (3× enemies, all bosses roam, no minimap, permadeath option) and its unlock deserves the strongest celebratory shake, matching boss-gauntlet-completion and prestige weight. Also persists immediately.

### Vault Puzzle Correct-Rune Juice (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **Camera trauma + sparkle on correct rune touch** — `notify_puzzle_rune_touched()` now triggers a small 0.08 camera trauma + a golden sparkle burst at the rune's position when the player touches the correct rune in the Simon-says puzzle. Previously each correct rune only brightened its emission + played a `SFX_DIALOGUE` blip — the puzzle felt weightless, with no physical feedback for correct steps. The light 0.08 trauma (matching craft-fail/buff-expire weight) ensures 4 rapid correct touches don't shake the screen excessively, while the sparkle gives each step a visible reward.

## Enhancement Pack 15 — Death Screen Stats + Score Milestones + Low-HP Heal Pulse

### Death Screen Additional Stats (`death_screen.gd`)
- **Level Reached, Final Biome, Game Mode** — the death screen now displays three additional stats that were already tracked by GameManager but never shown to the player. Previously the death screen showed only Final Score, Total Kills, Best Combo, Max Pickup Streak, and Time Survived — the player had no context about their run's progression level, where they died, or what game mode they were playing. Now the death screen shows Level Reached (from `GameManager.player_level`), Final Biome (from `GameManager.current_biome` via `GameConstants.BIOME_NAMES`), and Game Mode (from `GameModeManager.get_mode_name()`). The stat block was compacted slightly (line height 30→28, starting Y offset -40→-70) to fit the three new lines without overflowing the screen.

### Score Milestone Celebrations (`game_manager.gd`)
- **9 score milestone thresholds** — at 1K, 5K, 10K, 25K, 50K, 100K, 250K, 500K, and 1M cumulative score, the game now fires a "🏆 SCORE MILESTONE: [label]!" HUD message + scaled camera trauma + golden sparkle burst at the player's position. Previously score only incremented as a bare number with per-kill floating "+N" labels — the player had no sense of cumulative achievement milestones. The camera trauma scales with the milestone tier index (0.08 at 1K → 0.4 cap at 100K+), and higher-tier milestones (25K+, index 3+) also get an FOV kick (3°→6°) for a "rising tension" effect. Each threshold fires exactly once per run (tracked via `_last_score_milestone`, reset in `_start_game()`). The formatted label uses K/M suffixes (1K, 10K, 100K, 1M) for readability.

### Low-HP Heal Pulse (`game_manager.gd` + `particle_effects.gd`)
- **Green expanding ring + sparkles on emergency heals** — when the player heals from below 25% HP (the same threshold as the low-HP warning shader and heartbeat intensity), a green expanding ring + rising green sparkles + soft green OmniLight flash now radiate from the player's position. This gives emergency heals (health fragment pickup at critical HP, healing shrine activation) a visible "life saved" pulse that reinforces the danger the player was in. The effect uses `ParticleEffects.spawn_heal_pulse()` — a new particle function with a 60-particle green expanding ring (ease-out expansion matching the pulse wave/shockwave visual language), 40 rising green sparkles, and a 3.0-energy green light flash that fades over 0.3s. The green color is distinct from the level-up shockwave (gold) and combo fireworks (multi-color) so the player can identify the event by color alone. Skipped for normal heals above 25% HP to avoid visual spam.

## Enhancement Pack 16 — World Life & Companion Event Feedback Polish

### Pet Emote SFX (`companion_pet.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **Per-emote pitched SFX** — the pet emote system (HAPPY, LOVE, ANGRY, SCARED, CURIOUS, SLEEPY, HUNGRY) had visual feedback (Label3D pop-in + material flash) but no audio. Now each emote plays `SFX_PET_EMOTE` (a gentle 880 Hz sine blip) with a unique pitch per emotion: HAPPY=1.3× (bright), LOVE=1.1× (warm), ANGRY=0.6× (deep growl), SCARED=0.8× (trembling), CURIOUS=1.2× (questioning), SLEEPY=0.5× (low-long), HUNGRY=0.9× (pulsing). This gives the companion pet a sonic personality — the player can recognize their pet's emotional state by ear alone without looking at the emote bubble, reinforcing the companion bond. The pitch mapping is a Dictionary lookup in `_trigger_emote()` with a 1.0× fallback for NONE.

### Wandering Merchant Event SFX (`wandering_merchant.gd` + `wandering_merchant_spawner.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **Merchant spawn SFX** — `_spawn_merchant()` in the spawner now plays `SFX_MERCHANT` (ascending C5→E5→G5 major triad arpeggio) alongside the existing materialization particles + HUD message. Previously the merchant's arrival in the world was completely silent — the player had no audio cue that a rare trading opportunity had appeared, easy to miss during combat.
- **Merchant proximity SFX** — the "has arrived! Rare goods for sale nearby" proximity prompt in `wandering_merchant.gd` now plays `SFX_MERCHANT` at 0.8× pitch (lower than the spawn chime so the two events sound different: spawn = "someone arrived somewhere nearby", proximity = "they're right here, ready to trade"). Previously the proximity glow + prompt label pop-in was purely visual.
- **Merchant despawn SFX** — `_despawn()` now plays `SFX_MERCHANT` at 0.6× pitch (lowest, fading) so the player hears the merchant departing. Previously the merchant's departure — whether by wandering out of range or timed despawn — was silent, and the player might keep searching for a trader that had already left.

### Enemy Dodge Whoosh SFX (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **Dodge air-distortion SFX** — when an EVASIVE variant dodges incoming damage, `SFX_DODGE` (a quick 0.08s noise sweep at 0.18 volume) now plays alongside the existing "DODGE" Label3D text. Previously the dodge had only visual feedback — a grey "DODGE" text appeared above the enemy but the player's shot appeared to silently vanish. The whoosh timbre conveys air disturbance (something narrowly slipping past a projectile), distinct from the enemy hit blip (solid impact) and the shielded variant's blue shield flash (solid deflector thunk). The player can now identify an Evasive variant by ear after the first dodged shot.

### Enemy Near-Death Shudder SFX (`enemy_ai_controller.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)

## Enhancement Pack 17 — UI Panel Toggle & Interaction SFX Polish

### Quest Log Toggle SFX (`quest_log.gd`)
- **Open/close SFX** — pressing Tab to toggle the mission board now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.0× pitch (open) or 0.85× pitch (close). Previously the mission board appeared and disappeared silently — the player had no audio cue that the panel toggled. Matches the open/close audio language used across all UI panels (pause menu, photo mode, death screen).

### Leaderboards Tab Switch & Seed Action SFX (`leaderboards_page.gd`)
- **Close SFX** — pressing F12 to close the leaderboards now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously only the open had a click sound; closing was silent.
- **Tab switch SFX** — switching between the 6 leaderboard tabs (keys 1-6) now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at an ascending pitch per tab index (1.0× + 0.05× per tab), giving rapid tab cycling a gentle arpeggio feel. Previously tab switching was completely silent — the player pressed a number key and the content changed with no audio confirmation.
- **Seed apply success SFX** — successfully applying a challenge seed now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.2× pitch (bright, positive confirmation). Previously the seed was applied with only a text message.
- **Seed apply failure SFX** — entering an invalid seed now plays `SFX_CRAFT_FAIL` (the descending dissonant buzz used across all 6 crafting systems), for consistent negative-feedback language. Previously the invalid seed had only a text warning.
- **Seed copy SFX** — pressing S to copy the current seed to clipboard now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK`, confirming the clipboard action. Previously the seed was copied silently with only a text message.

### Statistics Page Tab Switch & Close SFX (`statistics_page.gd`)
- **Close SFX** — pressing F2 to close the statistics page now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously only the open had a click sound; closing was silent.
- **Tab switch SFX** — switching between the 4 statistics tabs (keys 1-4) now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at an ascending pitch per tab index, matching the leaderboards tab-switch audio language. Previously tab switching was completely silent.

### Mode Selector Hover SFX (`mode_selector.gd`)
- **Card hover SFX** — moving the mouse over a mode card now plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` (subtle blip) when entering a card. Previously the mode selector tracked hover state for visual highlighting but had no audio feedback — the player's cursor crossed card boundaries in silence. Only plays when entering a card (not when leaving to empty space) to avoid noise on every motion boundary crossing. Matches the Button-based menus' hover feedback.

### Skill Tree Hover & Close SFX (`skill_tree.gd`)
- **Close SFX** — pressing K to close the skill tree now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously only the open had a click sound; closing with the key was silent (the close button click had SFX but the keyboard toggle didn't).
- **Node hover SFX** — moving the mouse over a skill node now plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` when entering a node. Previously the skill tree tracked hover for visual highlighting but had no audio feedback. Matches the Button-based menus' hover feedback.

### Equipment Menu Tab Hover SFX (`equipment_menu.gd`)
- **Tab header hover SFX** — moving the mouse over a tab header (Equipment/Consumables/Refine/Materials) now plays `SFX_UI_HOVER`. Previously the equipment menu tracked hover state for all elements (tabs, pieces, consumables, refine recipes) but had no hover audio. Only tab headers get the SFX (not every list item) to avoid excessive noise when scrolling through the equipment list.

### Replay Browser Navigation & Action SFX (`replay_browser.gd`)
- **Close SFX** — pressing F11 to close the replay browser now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously only the open had a click sound; closing was silent.
- **List navigation SFX** — pressing Up/Down arrows to scroll through replays now plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` (soft blip), giving the list a responsive feel. Previously navigating the replay list was completely silent.
- **Play SFX** — pressing Enter to play a replay now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` (confirming action). Previously the replay started with only a text message.
- **Spectate SFX** — pressing S to spectate a replay now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK`. Previously spectating started silently.
- **Delete SFX** — pressing Delete to remove a replay now plays `SFX_CRAFT_FAIL` (the negative-action buzz), for consistent destructive-action feedback language. Previously the replay was deleted with only a text message.

### Fast Travel Menu Open/Close SFX (`fast_travel_menu.gd`)
- **Open SFX** — `open()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK`. Previously the fast travel menu opened silently despite having hover SFX for waypoint items.
- **Close SFX** — `close()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously the menu closed silently whether via close button, Esc, or Tab.

### Trade Menu Open/Close SFX (`trade_menu.gd`)
- **Open SFX** — `open()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK`. Previously the trade menu opened silently when the player pressed E near a trader — the player had no audio cue that the menu opened (only the visual fade-in).
- **Close SFX** — `close()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously the trade menu closed silently.

### Crafting Menu Toggle SFX (`crafting_menu.gd`)
- **Open/close SFX** — `_on_menu_toggled()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.0× (open) or 0.85× (close) pitch. Previously the crafting menu toggled silently when pressing C — it had an entrance animation (scale + fade) but no audio, and the close was completely silent.

### Weapon Mod Fusion Menu Open/Close SFX (`weapon_mod_fusion_menu.gd`)
- **Open SFX** — `show_menu()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK`. Previously opening the fusion submenu from the crafting menu was silent.
- **Close SFX** — `hide_menu()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch. Previously closing the fusion submenu was silent.

### Pet Accessory Menu Close Pitch Differentiation (`pet_accessory_menu.gd`)
- **Open/close pitch differentiation** — the toggle SFX now uses 1.0× pitch for open and 0.85× for close (previously both used the same 1.0× pitch, so opening and closing sounded identical). Esc-close now also plays the close SFX (previously Esc-close was silent).

### Pet Training Menu Close Pitch Differentiation (`pet_training_menu.gd`)
- **Open/close pitch differentiation** — the toggle SFX now uses 1.0× pitch for open and 0.85× for close (previously both used the same 1.0× pitch). Esc-close now also plays the close SFX (previously Esc-close was silent).

## Enhancement Pack 18 — System-Level SFX Coverage

### Scene Transition SFX (`scene_transition.gd`)
- **Scene change whoosh** — `change_scene()` and `fade_callback()` now play `SFX_RIFT` (soft whoosh) when a transition begins. Previously the fade-to-black was completely silent, making the transition feel like a technical hitch rather than a stylized cut. The whoosh gives the fade an audio identity so the player subconsciously registers "scene changing" even while looking at a black screen.

### Tutorial Step SFX (`tutorial_manager.gd`)
- **Step appearance chime** — `_show_step()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.15× pitch when a new tutorial tooltip appears. Previously the tooltip faded in silently and was easy to miss during combat. The gentle high chime draws the player's attention without being startling.
- **Step dismiss SFX** — `dismiss_current()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.85× pitch when the player clicks "Got it!" or "Skip Tutorial". Previously the "Got it!" button was completely silent — the player got no audio confirmation that their click registered.

### Cosmetic Skin & Trail SFX (`cosmetic_manager.gd`)
- **Skin change SFX** — `set_skin()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` when the active skin changes. Previously cycling skins with F10 was completely silent — the player had no audio cue that their cosmetic changed.
- **Trail style change SFX** — `set_trail_style()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.1× pitch. Previously trail style changes were silent.
- **Trail color change SFX** — `set_trail_color_index()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.2× pitch (slightly higher to distinguish from style change). Previously trail color changes were silent.
- **Skin unlock SFX** — `refresh_unlocks()` now plays `SFX_LEVEL_UP` (celebratory chime) when a skin's unlock criteria is met. Previously skin unlocks had only a HUD message — a significant milestone deserved a celebratory sound matching achievement unlocks.

### Accessibility Keyboard Shortcut SFX (`accessibility_manager.gd`)
- **Color filter cycle SFX** — F6 now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` when cycling color filters. Previously the filter change had only a HUD message.
- **Colorblind mode cycle SFX** — F7 now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.9× pitch (slightly lower to distinguish from filter). Previously the colorblind mode change was silent.
- **UI scale change SFX** — F8/Shift+F8 now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` with pitch 1.1× (scale up) or 0.9× (scale down) so the player hears the direction of the change. Previously UI scale changes were silent.

### Ghost Mode Toggle SFX (`ghost_mode.gd`)
- **Toggle SFX** — `toggle_ghost()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.0× (enable) or 0.85× (disable). Previously the ghost mode on/off was completely silent — the player had no audio cue that the toggle registered.

### Save System SFX (`save_system.gd`)
- **Auto-save chime** — `_do_autosave()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.7× pitch (very quiet) alongside the existing "💾 Saved" HUD message. Previously the auto-save was silent beyond the text — a barely-audible click lets the player subconsciously register that progress was saved without being distracted from gameplay.
- **Manual save SFX** — `save_game()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.8× pitch on successful save. Previously manual saves were silent.
- **Load SFX** — `load_game()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.2× pitch (higher than save to distinguish "restored" from "saved"). Previously loading a save was silent.

### Tooltip Appearance SFX (`tooltip_manager.gd`)
- **Tooltip show blip** — `_show()` now plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` at 0.7× pitch (very quiet) when a tooltip appears after the delay. Previously the tooltip faded in silently — a soft hover blip lets the player know information is available without being annoying during frequent hover changes.

### FPS Counter Toggle SFX (`fps_counter.gd`)
- **Toggle SFX** — F3 now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.0× (show) or 0.85× (hide). Previously the FPS overlay toggle was completely silent — the player had no audio cue that the key registered.

### Pet Stone Inventory SFX (`pet_stone_inventory.gd`)
- **Stone pickup SFX** — `add_stone()` now plays `SFX_PET` at 1.3× pitch (bright, rare-item feel) when a pet evolution stone is picked up. Previously the stone was added silently — the collectible SFX covers the pickup, but a dedicated chime reinforces the "rare item" feel.
- **Stone consume SFX** — `consume_stone()` now plays `SFX_PET` at 0.9× pitch (lower, consuming). Previously stone consumption was silent.

### World Modifier Roll SFX (`world_modifier_system.gd`)
- **Modifier roll SFX** — `roll_modifiers()` now plays `SFX_MUTATION` when world modifiers are rolled for a new run. Previously the modifier roll was completely silent — only a HUD message appeared. The mutation chime (already thematically linked to gameplay-altering effects) gives the roll an audio identity so the player knows this run has special rules.

### Control Rebind SFX (`control_rebind.gd`)
- **Rebind confirmation SFX** — `rebind_action()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.2× pitch (bright confirmation) when a new key is successfully bound. Previously the rebind was silent at the system level — the rebind menu plays its own SFX, but the system itself had no confirmation.
- **Reset to defaults SFX** — `reset_to_defaults()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.8× pitch (lower, reverting). Previously the reset was silent.

### Character Select SFX (`character_select_manager.gd`)
- **Character set SFX** — `set_character()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` when the selected character changes. Previously the character selection was silent at the system level — the character_select.gd UI plays its own SFX, but the system-level setter had no feedback.

### Performance Optimizer Quality Change SFX (`performance_optimizer.gd`)
- **Quality downgrade SFX** — `_evaluate_quality()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.7× pitch when auto-quality downgrades. Previously the visual quality change was silent — the player saw the game get blurmer without knowing why. The lower-pitched click conveys "adjusting down".
- **Quality upgrade SFX** — auto-quality upgrade plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 1.1× pitch (higher, positive). Previously quality upgrades were silent.

### Daily & Weekly Challenge Start SFX (`daily_challenge_system.gd` + `weekly_challenge_system.gd`)
- **Daily challenge start SFX** — `start_daily_attempt()` now plays `SFX_LEVEL_UP` (bright chime) when a daily challenge attempt begins. Previously the start of a special, one-attempt-per-day run was silent.
- **Weekly challenge start SFX** — `start_weekly_attempt()` now plays `SFX_LEVEL_UP` when a weekly challenge attempt begins. Previously the start of a limited-attempts-per-week run was silent.

### Procedural Boss Spawn SFX (`procedural_boss_generator.gd`)
- **Boss spawn SFX** — `generate_boss_at()` now plays `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN` when a procedural boss appears. Previously the procedural boss spawn had a HUD message but no audio — all other boss spawn paths (arena, world boss, mode-driven) already played `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN`, so this was an inconsistency.

### Replay System SFX (`replay_system.gd`)
- **Playback start SFX** — `play_replay()` now plays `SFX_RIFT` (soft whoosh) when a replay starts playing. Previously the ghost appeared silently.
- **Playback finish SFX** — `_finish_playback()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.8× pitch when a replay finishes. Previously the replay ended silently.

### Spectator Mode SFX (`spectator_mode.gd`)
- **Enter spectator SFX** — `start_spectating()` now plays `SFX_RIFT` (soft whoosh) when entering spectator mode. Previously entering spectator was silent.
- **Exit spectator SFX** — `stop_spectating()` now plays `SFX_UI_CLICK` at 0.8× pitch when exiting spectator mode. Previously exiting was silent.

### Leaderboard New Record SFX (`leaderboards.gd`)
- **New #1 record SFX** — `submit_score()` now plays `SFX_LEVEL_UP` (celebratory chime) when a submitted score becomes the new #1 record for its mode. Previously achieving a new top score had only a signal emission — the player had no audio feedback for hitting the top of the leaderboard.

## Enhancement Pack 19 — Pet Training, Arena Hazard & Shadow Clone Feedback Polish

### Pet Training Game Completion Juice (`pet_training_system.gd`)
- **Training success SFX + camera trauma + sparkle** — `_end_game(true)` now plays `SFX_COMBO_MILESTONE` (triumphant arpeggio) + 0.15 camera trauma + green sparkle on the player. Previously completing a pet training mini-game had only a text message — no audio, no physical feedback for a significant pet progression event.
- **Training failure SFX + camera trauma** — `_end_game(false)` now plays `SFX_CRAFT_FAIL` (descending dissonant buzz) + 0.08 camera trauma (lighter, negative feedback). Previously failing a training game had only a text message — the player got no sensory feedback for a failed attempt.

### Pet Training Dash Course Waypoint Hit Juice (`pet_training_system.gd`)
- **Waypoint hit camera trauma** — each waypoint hit in the Dash Course mini-game now triggers a gentle 0.04 camera trauma alongside the existing `SFX_PICKUP` + sparkle. Previously waypoint hits had audio + visual feedback but no physical feedback — the rings were collected without any tactile "pop". The 0.04 trauma is very gentle so hitting all 5 in quick succession doesn't shake the screen excessively.

### Pet Training Target Dodge Bolt Fire SFX (`pet_training_system.gd`)
- **Dummy bolt fire SFX** — `_fire_dummy_bolt()` now plays `SFX_SHOOT` at 0.7× pitch when a training dummy fires a bolt at the player. Previously the only warning was the visual bolt appearing — the player had no audio cue that a projectile was incoming. The lowered pitch distinguishes it from the player's own weapon fire so it reads as a training bolt, not a friendly shot.

### Arena Hazard Activation SFX (`arena_hazard.gd`)
- **Activation SFX per hazard type** — `_activate()` now plays a distinct SFX per hazard type matching the environmental_hazard audio language: LAVA_GEYSER → `SFX_EXPLOSION`, FALLING_CRYSTAL → `SFX_BREAKABLE`, VOID_SHOCKWAVE → `SFX_RIFT`. Previously arena hazards had particles + camera shake on activation but no audio — the telegraph SFX (SFX_RIFT) was the only sound, and the actual eruption/impact was silent. The environmental hazards already had per-type activation SFX but arena hazards were left out.

### Shadow Clone Combat Feedback (`shadow_clone.gd`)
- **Shoot SFX** — `_shoot_dark_projectile()` now plays `SFX_SHOOT` at 0.6× pitch (deep, ominous void bolt launch). Previously the shadow clone's dark projectile attack had a visual scale pulse but no audio — the Void dimension mini-boss was shooting in silence. The lowered pitch distinguishes it from the player's weapon.
- **Hit SFX** — `take_damage_from()` now plays `SFX_ENEMY_HIT` at 0.7× pitch (heavier, boss-like). The shadow clone extends `CharacterBody3D` directly (not `EnemyBase`), so it doesn't inherit the `SFX_ENEMY_HIT` from `enemy_base.gd`'s `take_damage_from()`. Previously hitting the shadow clone had a visual emission flash but no audio — every other enemy in the game plays a hit sound when damaged.
- **Hit squash** — `take_damage_from()` now triggers a quick body mesh scale pop (1.25× → 1.0× with `TRANS_ELASTIC` rebound) matching the `enemy_base.gd` hit squash for combat feedback parity. Previously the shadow clone's hit reaction was emission-only — no physical "punch" on impact.

## Enhancement Pack 20 — Near-Miss Graze + Boss Intro Card + Crit Flash

### Near-Miss Graze SFX (`enemy_projectile.gd` + `audio_manager.gd` + `game_constants.gd`)
- **New SFX_GRAZE** — a subtle, very short airy whoosh (0.06s noise sweep at 0.12 volume) that plays when an enemy projectile passes close to the player without hitting — the audio cue for "that was close!" Shorter and quieter than SFX_DODGE (0.08s, 0.18) so multiple simultaneous grazes during projectile-heavy encounters (Spore Spitters, Crystal Guardians, Drakes, Mirror Mimics) don't stack into noise. The noise-sweep timbre conveys air disturbance — the bolt's wake brushing past the player's body.
- **Graze detection** — `_physics_process` in `enemy_projectile.gd` now checks if the projectile is within `ENEMY_PROJECTILE_GRAZE_RADIUS` (2.8m) but outside `ENEMY_PROJECTILE_HIT_RADIUS` (1.5m) of the player. This creates a ~1.3m "graze band" where the SFX triggers. Each projectile can only graze once (flagged via `_has_grazed`) so a bolt lingering near the player doesn't replay the SFX. Co-op aware — P2 gets the same graze detection using the shared `_has_grazed` flag so the projectile only grazes once total (either player).
- **New constant** — `ENEMY_PROJECTILE_GRAZE_RADIUS` (2.8) in `game_constants.gd`.

### Boss Intro Card (`boss_intro_card.gd` + `hud.gd`)
- **Dramatic full-screen boss name overlay** — `boss_intro_card.gd` (Control added to HUD) shows the boss's name in large gold text (36pt) centered on a dark semi-transparent panel with a red border, with a subtitle tag ("⚠ BOSS", "☠ MEGA-BOSS" for HP ≥ 500, "🌍 WORLD BOSS" for world bosses). The card slides in from the left with ease-out-back overshoot (0.35s), holds for 1.6s, then slides out to the right with ease-in-cubic (0.35s). A semi-transparent dark backdrop fades in/out with the card for contrast.
- **Cinematic boss appearances** — previously boss spawns had a boss HP bar slide-in + HUD message + SFX_BOSS_SPAWN, but no dramatic "title card" moment. The intro card gives boss appearances a cinematic beat — the player sees the boss's name in large text centered on screen, creating an anticipation moment before the fight begins. The card is purely visual — doesn't block input or pause the game. Coexists with the boss HP bar entrance animation (which slides down from above) and the HUD message.

### Critical Hit Screen-Edge Flash (`crit_flash.gd` + `hud.gd` + `game_manager.gd` + `projectile.gd`)
- **New `critical_hit` signal** — `GameManager.critical_hit(mod_color: Color)` signal emitted by `projectile.gd` when a crit lands. The mod_color parameter lets the flash be tinted to the weapon mod's color for thematic variety.
- **Gold screen-edge glow on crits** — `crit_flash.gd` (Control added to HUD) listens to `critical_hit` and plays a brief gold-colored screen-edge glow. The flash color is a blend of 60% gold + 40% the weapon mod's color, so crits always read as "golden" while fire mods flash orange, ice mods flash cyan, void mods flash purple, etc. The flash has a sharp onset (first 15% of the 0.30s duration, ease-out quadratic) then a gentle fade (remaining 85%, ease-in cubic) — reading as a "lingering glow" rather than a linear drain. Max alpha 0.22 (subtle but visible).
- **Cooldown** — 0.08s cooldown prevents rapid-fire crits from stacking the flash into a persistent glow. The first crit flashes, subsequent crits within the cooldown are suppressed.
- **New visual feedback layer** — previously crits had the gold damage number + hit-stop freeze + SFX_CRIT_HIT, but no screen-level visual effect. The screen-edge flash adds a fourth feedback channel — a peripheral glow that the player registers subconsciously, reinforcing the "that was a good hit" feedback loop without requiring the player to look at the damage number.

## Enhancement Pack 21 — Environmental & Combat Action SFX

### Destructible Crate Shatter SFX (`destructible.gd`)
- **Shatter SFX** — the destructible crate shatter had particles + light flash + camera shake + hit-stop but no audio. The existing `SFX_BREAKABLE` noise hit (0.25s, 0.45 vol) — already used for breakable walls and crystal wraith shards — now also plays when destructible crates shatter. Previously crate destruction was completely silent, creating an inconsistency with breakable walls (which played `SFX_BREAKABLE` on shatter) and a jarring gap between the visual spectacle (25-particle explosion + light flash + camera shake + hit-stop freeze) and the complete absence of audio. The noise-hit timbre reads as a physical crack/break rather than a tonal effect.

### Graviton Pull Activation SFX (`enemy_graviton.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_PULL** — a deep descending gravitational whomp (90→45 Hz, 0.30s, 0.40 vol) that plays when the Graviton activates its gravity pull. The low-frequency sweep conveys a massive force engaging — the sound of space being bent. Added to the `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` list for natural micro-detuning.
- **Pull activation wiring** — the Graviton's gravity pull activation had a visual warning ring (appears on the ground 1.5s before pull) + emission brightening but no audio when the pull actually engaged. The player had no audio cue that the pull was active until they were already being dragged toward the Graviton. Now the SFX_PULL whomp fires at the exact moment `pull_active = true`, giving the player an audio cue to start dodging.

### Wildlife Flee Start SFX (`wildlife.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_WILDLIFE_FLEE** — a tiny startled chirp (1400 Hz, 0.05s, 0.10 vol) that plays once when a wildlife creature transitions from wandering to fleeing. The short, high sound reads as a small animal's startled reaction — the audio cue for "it saw you and is running." Intentionally very quiet (0.10 vol) so it doesn't compete with combat sounds.
- **Flee transition wiring** — the flee state had only a visual emission brightening (1.2→2.0 emission_energy_multiplier) but no audio. The player could walk past wildlife without realizing they'd scared it. The SFX fires only on the wander→flee transition (not every frame while fleeing) so a single encounter produces exactly one chirp.

### Player Landing SFX (`player.gd` + `player2_zerp.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_LAND** — a low thud (0.10s noise hit, 0.25 vol) for when Zorp touches down after being airborne (reverse-gravity exit, bounce pad landing, big fall). Added to the `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` list for natural micro-detuning.
- **Fall-distance pitch scaling** — the landing SFX pitch scales down with fall distance: a gentle 2m hop plays at 1.2× pitch (light, barely audible), a 10m+ slam plays at 0.7× pitch (deep, solid thump). This mirrors the existing fall-distance scaling on the squash intensity (0.6→1.8), dust size (0.6× intensity), and camera shake (0.12× intensity) — all four feedback channels now scale together. Uses `play_sfx_pitched()` for explicit pitch control.
- **P2 parity** — `player2_zerp.gd` `_play_landing_effect()` also plays `SFX_LAND` (at fixed 1.0× pitch since P2's landing effect doesn't track fall distance), maintaining co-op audio parity.

### Monolith Buff Activation Dedicated SFX (`monolith.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_BUFF_ACTIVATE** — a warm ascending C major arpeggio (C5→E5→G5→C6, 0.06s per note, 0.30 vol) distinct from `SFX_HEAL` (two-note G5→B5 chime). The ascending major triad is the universal audio shorthand for a positive status change in games — conveying "you just got powered up" rather than "you just got healed."
- **Monolith buff activation wiring** — monolith buffs (Speed Surge, Power Surge, Wisdom Aura) previously used the generic `SFX_HEAL` sound. The distinction matters because monolith buffs are offensive/utility buffs (speed multiplier, damage multiplier, XP multiplier), not healing — the audio should match the intent. The 4-note ascending arpeggio is more triumphant than the 2-note heal chime, matching the visual buff-activation effect (upward beam of particles + light flash + screen shake) that already existed.

## Enhancement Pack 22 — Debug Print Cleanup + README Accuracy

### Debug print() → print_verbose() Production Cleanup (32 scripts, 74 calls)
- **All 74 `print()` debug log calls converted to `print_verbose()`** across 32 GDScript files: `balance_manager.gd`, `boss_arena.gd`, `character_select_manager.gd`, `co_op_manager.gd`, `companion_pet.gd`, `cosmetic_manager.gd`, `daily_challenge_system.gd`, `death_replay.gd`, `dungeon_generator.gd`, `endgame_manager.gd`, `enemy_spawner.gd`, `game_manager.gd`, `game_mode_manager.gd`, `intro_cinematic.gd`, `leaderboards.gd`, `multi_pet_system.gd`, `navigation_manager.gd`, `performance_optimizer.gd`, `pet_fusion_system.gd`, `pet_questline.gd`, `photo_mode.gd`, `player.gd`, `procedural_biome_generator.gd`, `progression_system.gd`, `replay_system.gd`, `spectator_mode.gd`, `statistics.gd`, `wandering_merchant_spawner.gd`, `weekly_challenge_system.gd`, `world_boss_manager.gd`, `world_generator.gd`, `world_modifier_system.gd`. `print_verbose()` only outputs when the engine is running with verbose mode enabled (–verbose flag or debug builds), keeping the production console clean while preserving all debug logging for development. This is a Phase 35 (Final Polish) code cleanup item — the codebase had accumulated 74 developer `print()` calls across 32 files over 22 enhancement packs, which would spam the output console in production/release builds.

### README Accuracy Fixes (`README.md`)
- **Enemy count corrected** — "24+ enemy types" → "21 enemy types" (matching the actual `EnemyType` enum count: 8 original + 3 Enhancement Pack + 10 Phase 23 = 21)
- **Weapon mod count corrected** — "30+ weapon mods" → "36 weapon mods" in the combat system line, and "34 craftable weapon mods" → "35 craftable weapon mods" in the weapon mod crafting feature line (matching the actual `WeaponMod` enum: 35 craftable mods + 1 NONE = 36 total; mind control dart was the 35th craftable mod added in Phase 24 but the README count was not updated)

## Enhancement Pack 23 — Loot Drop Visual & Audio Feedback

### Rare Material Drop Feedback (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_RARE_DROP** — a shimmering ascending chime (E5→G5→B5→E6, 659→784→988→1319 Hz, 0.06s per note, 0.25 vol) that plays when a rare crafting material drops from an enemy. Rare materials are added directly to inventory (no physical collectible), so previously the only feedback was a text message — easy to miss during combat. The chime is pitched higher than SFX_PICKUP_RARE (which plays on physical collectible pickup) so the player can distinguish "something valuable dropped from this enemy" from "I picked up a rare item." Uses an augmented fifth arpeggio (E-G-B-E) for a magical, valuable feel. Added to the `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` list for natural micro-detuning.
- **Color-matched particle burst** — `ParticleEffects.spawn_pickup_sparkle()` in the rare material's theme color (from `RARE_MATERIAL_COLORS`) at the enemy's position + 1m height, so the player sees exactly where the rare drop happened.
- **Color-matched OmniLight flash** — a brief OmniLight3D (4.0 energy, 6m range) in the rare material's theme color flashes at the enemy's position and fades over 0.4s (ease-out), giving the drop visibility in dark biomes where the sparkle alone might be hard to see. The light is self-managing (tween → queue_free).

### Pet Evolution Stone Drop Feedback (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_PET_STONE_DROP** — a warm magical chime (A4→C5→E5, 440→523→659 Hz, 0.12s, 0.22 vol) that plays when a pet evolution stone drops from an enemy. Pet stones are rare (1.5% normal, 100% boss) and the player should know immediately when one drops so they can spot the collectible on the ground. The major triad conveys the mystical nature of evolution stones. Quieter than SFX_RARE_DROP since the collectible itself also plays SFX_PICKUP_RARE when picked up — the drop SFX is a "look over there" cue, not a "you got it" cue. Added to the `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` list.
- **Color-matched sparkle burst** — `ParticleEffects.spawn_pickup_sparkle()` in the stone's theme color (from `PET_STONE_COLORS` indexed by the PetPath the stone maps to) at the enemy's position + 1m height, drawing the player's attention to the drop location.

### Crafting Material Drop Feedback (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_CRAFT_DROP** — a subtle material-friendly blip (880 Hz, 0.04s, 0.12 vol) that plays when a crafting material drops from an enemy. Quieter and shorter than the rare drop and pet stone sounds since crafting materials are common (12% drop rate) — playing a loud sound on every material drop would be noisy during heavy combat. Boss drops play at 0.9× pitch (slightly lower, more substantial) to differentiate guaranteed boss drops from random common drops. Added to the `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` list.
- **Color-matched sparkle** — `ParticleEffects.spawn_pickup_sparkle()` in the crafting material's color (from the collectible's `TYPE_CONFIG`) at the enemy's position + 0.8m height, giving a subtle visual cue that something shiny dropped.

## Enhancement Pack 24 — Enemy Stagger + Dash Exhaustion + Magnet Hum

### Enemy Attack Windup Interruption with Stagger (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **Attack interruption** — when an enemy takes damage during its attack windup, the windup is now cancelled and the attack does NOT fire. Previously, taking damage during windup played a hit flash but the attack still went through after the windup timer — hitting a charging enemy felt pointless because it couldn't actually prevent the attack. Now hitting an enemy mid-windup rewards aggressive play: the player can prevent incoming attacks by dealing damage fast enough.
- **Stagger visual** — the interrupted enemy plays a distinct recoil animation: the body_mesh briefly squashes horizontally and stretches vertically (as if knocked backward), then bounces back with an elastic wobble (0.22s). This is visually different from the normal hit squash (which pops outward uniformly) so the player can distinguish "I hit it" from "I interrupted its attack." The `self.scale` (which was being squashed by the windup tween) is smoothly restored to `base_scale` via a separate tween.
- **Stagger SFX** — new `SFX_STAGGER` (660→440 Hz descending metallic ping, 0.10s, 0.25 vol) plays on stagger. The descending pitch reads as "recoil" — the enemy's force being deflected. Pitch scales with enemy size (larger enemies get a deeper stagger sound, matching the existing size-based hit pitch scaling). Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning.
- **Windup scale tween tracking** — both `_try_attack()` and `_try_attack_enemy()` now store their windup scale tween in `_windup_scale_tween` so it can be killed cleanly on interruption. Without this, the interrupted tween would still call `_execute_attack` after the stagger, defeating the interruption. The tween reference is cleared in `_execute_attack()` and `_execute_attack_on_enemy()` on normal completion.
- **Boss immunity** — bosses (max_hp >= 200) are immune to stagger — their attacks cannot be interrupted, only damaged through. This prevents stagger from trivializing boss fights where interrupting every attack would make bosses harmless.
- **Anti-spam** — after a stagger, `is_attacking` is reset after a 0.3s delay (not instantly) to prevent stagger → instant re-windup loops that would make the enemy stutter in place.
- **Dead code cleanup** — removed the unused `hit_flinch_timer` variable (declared at line 45, decremented at line 1467, but never set to any value anywhere — pure dead code since the original Phase 2 implementation).

### Player Dash Exhaustion Visual (`player.gd`)
- **Emission dimming on cooldown** — when dash is on cooldown, Zorp's emission dims by up to 20% and shifts toward a cool blue tint (0.3, 0.5, 0.8), visually communicating "recharging energy" — the alien equivalent of catching your breath. Previously the player had a HUD cooldown ring and a ready chime, but no on-character visual feedback during the cooldown — the 3D model looked identical whether dash was available or cooling down.
- **Recovery arc** — the exhaustion is strongest right after a dash (full dim) and fades smoothly as the cooldown progresses, reaching zero at 50% through the cooldown. This creates a recovery arc rather than a binary toggle — the player sees Zorp gradually "recover" rather than abruptly snapping back to normal. The blend is smoothed via exponential lerp so it doesn't snap.
- **Composition with other systems** — the exhaustion effect is skipped when the low-HP heartbeat is active (that system owns emission at critical HP), and when dashing/sliding/invuln-blinking (those own mesh scale and emission). It composes cleanly with the idle breathing emission pulse (which runs first, then the exhaustion dimming is applied as a post-multiplier) and the shoot/damage emission spikes (which set emission independently and override the dimming during their duration).
- **Cooldown fraction awareness** — the effect correctly accounts for the ProgressionSystem dash cooldown multiplier (Fluid Motion skill reduces cooldown), computing the full cooldown duration to calculate the fraction remaining, so the exhaustion arc is proportional to the actual cooldown time regardless of skill investment.

### Collectible Magnet Hum Audio (`collectible.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_MAGNET_HUM** — a very short (0.08s) soft sine pulse at 440 Hz (0.08 vol) that plays periodically while a collectible is being magnetically vacuumed toward the player. The warm mid-range frequency reads as a gentle "energy field" hum — the audio cue for "items are being pulled toward me." Very quiet (0.08 vol, played at 0.5 volume multiplier = 0.04 effective) so multiple simultaneous vacuums don't stack into noise. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural variation.
- **Throttled playback** — the hum plays every 0.18s (MAGNET_HUM_INTERVAL) while being pulled, not every frame. The interval is long enough that 5-10 simultaneous vacuuming items produce a gentle rhythmic hum rather than audio spam, but short enough to feel like a continuous energy field. Both the normal magnetic pull and the emergency health fragment magnet trigger the hum.
- **Previously missing** — the magnet pull had a visual sparkle trail and a magnet activation flash but no audio. The player could see items being pulled but heard nothing — the vacuum was completely silent. The hum adds an audio dimension to the magnetic pull, making it feel like an active energy effect rather than items just silently gliding toward the player.

## Enhancement Pack 25 — Weather Crossfade + Enemy Spawn Ring + Level-Up Golden Beam

### Weather Particle & Light Crossfade (`weather_system.gd`)
- **Weather particle crossfade** — old weather particles (rain, snow, embers, sandstorm, etc.) now fade out over 0.8s with ease-out cubic instead of vanishing in a single frame via `queue_free()`. The `emitting` flag is set to false immediately so no new particles spawn, and the existing particles' color alpha is tweened to 0 so they become transparent as they drift away. This prevents the jarring "pop" where 500 rain particles vanish in one frame only to be replaced by 500 snow particles — the old particles linger briefly and fade, creating a crossfade with the new weather's particles that are spawned immediately after. Previously weather transitions had a 4s fog crossfade and a camera trauma bump but the particles themselves hard-swapped, which undercut the smooth transition.
- **Solar/aurora light fade-out** — the shared solar/aurora light now fades its `light_energy` to 0 over 0.6s (ease-out quadratic) instead of `queue_free()`-ing instantly. Previously the orange solar flare light or green aurora light would pop out the instant the weather changed.
- **Phase 28 weather lights fade-out** — all 6 Phase 28 weather expansion lights (blood moon, eclipse, pollen, magnetic, gravity, dimensional) now fade out over 0.5s (ease-out quadratic) via the new `_fade_weather_light_node()` helper instead of instant `queue_free()`. Previously every weather transition involving Phase 28 weather types (blood moon, eclipse, magnetic storm, gravity anomaly, dimensional storm, pollen storm) would pop out the colored ambient light instantly, creating a visible "light switch" effect. Gameplay effects (EMP disable, gravity shifts) are still disabled immediately via the state flags — only the visual fades.
- **Combo weather lights fade-out** — the combo weather's lights (magnetic, gravity, dimensional, pollen) now also fade out over 0.4s via `_fade_weather_light_node()` when the combo ends, matching the primary weather light fade for a consistent transition. Previously combo weather light removal was instant, creating a secondary "pop" on top of the primary weather pop.

### Enemy Spawn Ground Ring (`enemy_spawner.gd` + `particle_effects.gd`)
- **Two-stage materialization** — enemies now get a small, quick ground ring at the exact spawn point the moment they materialize (before the 2s grace period fade-in), creating a two-stage visual: "energy arriving" (new initial ring) → "entity activated" (existing activation ring fired when the grace period ends). The initial ring is smaller (max_radius 1.5-4.0, scaled by enemy base_scale) and uses the existing `spawn_spawn_ring()` function with the enemy's base color. Previously the spawn ring only fired when the enemy became active 2s after spawning — the initial materialization had converging particles + a fade-in but no ground impact, making it look like the enemy was assembling in mid-air without touching the ground.

### Level-Up Golden Light Beam (`particle_effects.gd`)
- **Golden vertical light pillar** — level-ups now spawn a 12m golden light beam at the player's position using the existing `spawn_sky_beam()` helper (already used for boss deaths and rare pickups) with golden coloring (Color(1.0, 0.85, 0.2)). The beam is shorter than boss death beams (12m vs 30m) so it reads as a personal celebration, not a world-shaking event. The beam fades in fast (0.15s), holds (0.4s), then fades out (0.6s) — a 1.15s total duration matching the level-up freeze-frame (80ms) + shockwave expansion.
- **Golden OmniLight flash** — a golden OmniLight3D (6.0 energy, 12m range, warm gold color) flashes at the player's position and fades over 0.8s (ease-out quadratic), illuminating the surroundings so the level-up is visible even in dark biomes (underground, eclipse, digital grid). Brighter and wider than the enemy death flash (3.0+scale*2.0 energy, 4.0+scale*3.0 range) because level-up is a positive, celebratory moment. Previously level-ups had a golden expanding ring + upward sparkles + camera trauma + FOV kick + freeze-frame but no vertical light element — the celebration was horizontal, not vertical, missing the classic "power surge" beam that makes level-ups feel triumphant in action games.

## Enhancement Pack 26 — Mutation Reset Bug Fix + Endgame State Reset + Missing Event SFX + Dead Code Cleanup

### Mutation System Reset on Game Restart (`mutation_system.gd`)
- **Bug fix: mutations persisting across runs** — the MutationSystem had no `game_restarted` signal handler. When the player died and restarted, `_active_mutations` and `_biome_time` were never cleared, so mutations from the previous run could persist into the new run if the player spawned in the same biome. This was a stale state leak — the player could start a new run with fire resistance, damage reduction, or other mutation bonuses from the previous run's biome exposure. Now `_on_game_restarted()` properly removes all mutation visuals from players, clears the `_active_mutations` dictionary, resets `_biome_time` to 0, and resets `_current_biome` to -1 so the system cleanly re-initializes on the first biome change of the new run.

### Endgame Manager Full State Reset (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **Bug fix: gauntlet/survival progress fields not reset** — `_on_game_restarted()` was only resetting the top-level active/completed boolean flags (`_survival_active`, `_gauntlet_active`, `_boss_gauntlet_active`, etc.) but not the progress fields (`_gauntlet_index`, `_gauntlet_biome_timer`, `_gauntlet_kills_this_biome`, `_gauntlet_total_time`, `_boss_gauntlet_index`, `_boss_gauntlet_intermission`, `_boss_gauntlet_total_time`, `_survival_next_boss_time`, `_survival_score_accumulator`). These fields ARE properly initialized when `start_gauntlet()`/`start_boss_gauntlet()`/`start_survival()` is called, so the stale values would be overwritten on mode re-entry — but between restart and mode start, HUD elements could read stale values. Now all progress fields are explicitly zeroed in `_on_game_restarted()` for clean state.

### Missing Event SFX Coverage (8 scripts)
- **Sentinel shockwave SFX** (`enemy_sentinel.gd`) — `_fire_shockwave()` now plays `SFX_RIFT` on fire. The Sentinel's shockwave attack had visual particles + a light flash but no audio. The rift whoosh conveys a seismic energy ripple.
- **Spore Spitter spit SFX** (`enemy_spitter.gd`) — `_fire_spit()` now plays `SFX_SHOOT_POISON` on fire. The Spore Spitter's projectile attack was completely silent, making it hard to notice the projectile in busy combat. The acid hiss conveys the spore/spit nature of the attack.
- **Pulse wave SFX** (`pulse_wave.gd`) — `_ready()` now plays `SFX_PULSE_WAVE` on spawn. The player's Q ability had visual particles + light flash but no audio. The sweeping energy wave sound matches the expanding ring visual.
- **Shockwave ring SFX** (`shockwave.gd`) — `_ready()` now plays `SFX_EXPLOSION` on spawn. The Sentinel's shockwave ring had a light flash + expanding ring visual but no audio. The explosion conveys a seismic detonation matching the visual impact.
- **Boss intro card SFX** (`boss_intro_card.gd`) — `_show_card()` now plays `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN` synced with the card slide-in. The dramatic boss name card animation had a visual slide-in but no audio. The boss spawn sound syncs with the card appearance for a cinematic moment.
- **Pet evolution SFX** (`companion_hud.gd`) — `_on_stage_changed()` now plays `SFX_PET_EVOLVE`. The pet evolution milestone had only a visual border flash. The magical ascending chime conveys the pet's growth moment.
- **Trader SFX** (`trader.gd`) — `_try_trade()` now plays `SFX_MERCHANT` on successful trade. The trader exchange had only a camera shake. The merchant chime confirms the trade.
- **Co-op downed/revived/died SFX** (`co_op_hud.gd`) — `_on_p2_downed()` plays `SFX_DEATH` at 1.3× pitch (urgent), `_on_p2_revived()` plays `SFX_REVIVE` (warm rescue chime), `_on_p2_died()` plays `SFX_DEATH` (full death), `_on_p1_downed()` plays `SFX_DEATH` at 1.2× pitch. Previously these critical co-op events had no audio at all — the player had no audio cue when their partner went down, was revived, or died.

### Dead Code Cleanup (`weather_system.gd`)
- **Removed `_thunder_flash_light`** — the variable `var _thunder_flash_light: OmniLight3D = null` was declared at line 51 but never assigned, read, or cleaned up anywhere in the 1437-line file. It was a leftover from an earlier design where thunderstorm lightning flashes used a persistent light; the current implementation creates ad-hoc flash lights as local variables in `_execute_lightning_strike()`. Pure dead code — removed.

## Enhancement Pack 27 — P2 Walk Bob Parity + Heal Pitch Scaling + Enemy SFX Distance Attenuation

### P2 Walk Bob + Footstep SFX Parity (`player2_zerp.gd`)
- **Walk bob animation** — P2 (Zerp) previously only had the idle sine bob (a subtle 0.04m vertical oscillation at 2.5 rad/s). P1 has a speed-scaled walk bob that blends from idle to a faster |sin| hump pattern as the player moves faster, giving each step a distinct visual "footstep" bounce. Now P2 gets the same walk bob blend: `_walk_phase` advances proportional to speed, `_walk_blend` eases from idle to walk via exponential lerp, and the mesh y-position is a blended combination of the idle sine and the walk |sin| humps. The walk bob amplitude (0.07m) and speed (11.0 rad/s) match P1's constants exactly so co-op movement feels consistent. Previously P2's mesh only bobbed at the idle rate even when sprinting — a visual asymmetry that made P2 feel less alive than P1.
- **Footstep SFX** — P1 plays a soft procedural footstep thud (SFX_FOOTSTEP) at each walk bob hump valley (the zero-crossing of the underlying sine), with frequency naturally scaling by speed. P2 had no footstep audio at all — Zerp ran across the terrain in complete silence. Now P2 gets the same footstep SFX at the same hump-valley detection points, so co-op movement has parity audio. The threshold (walk_blend > 0.35, horiz_speed > 1.5 m/s) matches P1 so slow drift is silent for both players.

### Heal SFX Pitch Scaling (`game_manager.gd`)
- **Heal size → pitch mapping** — the heal SFX (SFX_HEAL, a two-note G5→B5 chime) previously played at a fixed 1.0× pitch regardless of heal amount. A small heal (health fragment pickup, +10 HP) sounded identical to a large heal (healing shrine, level-up heal, +80 HP). Now the pitch scales linearly with heal size: 1.2× at 10 HP (quick, bright tinkle — a small top-up) down to 0.85× at 80+ HP (lower, warmer recovery — a substantial heal). The mapping is clamped to [0.85, 1.2] so the range stays musical. This gives the player an audio cue for how much they healed without looking at the HP bar — a small heal reads as a quick "tink" while a big heal reads as a satisfying "ahh." Uses `play_sfx_pitched()` for explicit pitch control.

### Enemy Alert SFX Distance Attenuation (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **Alert SFX volume scales with distance** — the enemy alert blip (SFX_ENEMY_ALERT, a descending 440→880 Hz sweep) previously played at full volume regardless of how far the enemy was from the player. An enemy spotting the player from 50m away was just as loud as one right next to them, which was disorienting during large encounters with many simultaneous alerts. Now the alert SFX uses `play_sfx_volume()` with a smoothstep distance attenuation: full volume at ≤15m, 15% volume at 60m+, matching the existing spawn SFX distance model. Applied to both alert paths: the normal player-detection alert and the mind-control-target alert.
- **New `_compute_dist_atten()` helper** — a shared distance attenuation function that returns a volume multiplier (0.15–1.0) based on distance. Uses the same inverted smoothstep as the spawn SFX distance attenuation: `clampf((dist - 15) / 45, 0, 1)` → smoothstep → lerp(0.15, 1.0). Shared by the alert, hit, and death SFX paths.

### Enemy Hit SFX Distance Attenuation (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **Hit SFX volume scales with distance** — the enemy hit SFX (SFX_ENEMY_HIT) previously played at full volume regardless of distance. During combat with enemies at various ranges, a far-off hit was as loud as a point-blank one, creating audio clutter. Now the hit SFX uses `play_sfx_pitched_volume()` with the same `_compute_dist_atten()` distance model, composing the existing size-based pitch scaling with the new distance-based volume scaling. Close hits (≤15m) are full volume; distant hits (60m+) are 15% volume. This means the player hears their close-range combat clearly while far-off fights (e.g. pet attacking a distant enemy, mind-controlled enemy fighting allies) are subtler — matching the visual distance attenuation already applied to the camera shake.

### Enemy Death SFX Distance Attenuation (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **Death SFX volume scales with distance** — the enemy death SFX (SFX_ENEMY_DEATH) previously played at full volume regardless of distance, same as the hit SFX. Now uses `play_sfx_pitched_volume()` with the `_compute_dist_atten()` model, composing the existing size-based death pitch with distance-based volume. A close kill is a full-volume boom; a distant kill is a subtle 15% volume pop. This matches the existing distance attenuation on the death camera shake (which already uses the same smoothstep model) so the audio and visual distance cues are consistent.

## Enhancement Pack 28 — Spiral Vacuum + Afterimage Emission Fade + Boss Bar Enrage + First-Hit Aggro

### Collectible Spiral Vacuum Orbit (`collectible.gd`)
- **Spiral orbit during magnetic pull** — collectibles being magnetically pulled toward the player now spiral around the pull direction (player → item axis) rather than flying in a straight line. The spiral uses a perpendicular vector to the pull direction, with an orbit radius proportional to distance (tightens as the item closes in) and a phase that advances faster when closer (mimicking angular momentum conservation). Each collectible gets a random initial phase so multiple simultaneous vacuuming items don't spiral in sync — each one whirls into the player's magnetic field at its own angle, creating a dynamic "energy vortex" visual rather than items sliding on rails. The spiral applies to both the normal magnetic pull and the emergency health fragment magnet. Previously, items pulled in a straight horizontal line with only a subtle vertical lift arc — the spiral adds a whirling dimension that makes the magnetic vacuum feel like an active energy field rather than a simple gravitational pull.

### Dash Afterimage Emission Fade (`player.gd`)
- **Ghost emission fades alongside alpha** — dash afterimage ghosts now fade their `emission_energy_multiplier` to 0 over the lifetime alongside the alpha fade, so the ghost dims its glow as it becomes transparent — a cohesive "energy dissipating" read. Previously, the ghost's emission stayed at full intensity while the body faded to transparent, creating a "glowing outline shrinking" effect where the trail color's emission persisted at full strength while the body became invisible. This looked like a visual bug rather than a smooth energy dissolution. Now the alpha and emission fade together via parallel tweens with the same ease-out quadratic curve, so the ghost reads as cleanly dissipating energy — the glow softens as the body disperses. Applies to all 5 trail styles (Classic, Spark, Comet, Glitch, Aurora) since they all use the same fade tween pipeline.

### Boss HP Bar Enrage Glow Pulse (`hud.gd`)
- **Red border pulse when boss HP < 25%** — the boss HP bar container Panel now pulses with a red-tinted `self_modulate` glow when the boss drops below 25% HP (the enrage threshold matching the EnemyAIController enrage at <25% HP). The pulse is a 2.5 Hz sine wave whose amplitude scales with enrage depth — subtle at exactly 25% HP, urgent at 5% HP. The glow is applied via `self_modulate` so it doesn't interfere with the container's `modulate` (used by the entrance/exit fade animations). The red tint (1.0, 0.2, 0.15) with a modulating alpha >1.0 brightens the border and name text rhythmically, creating a "danger" warning that draws the eye without requiring the player to actively read the bar color. The pulse resets cleanly when the boss heals above the threshold or when the boss is defeated/removed. Previously, the boss bar had a damage flash + horizontal shake on hits and a smooth color lerp from green → red, but no persistent visual cue for the enrage state — the player had to notice the red bar color to know the boss was in its dangerous phase. The enrage pulse adds a UI-level heartbeat that makes the final phase of a boss fight feel tense.

### Enemy First-Hit Aggro Flash (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **Aggro ring + emission spike on first damage** — when an enemy is hit for the first time (transitions from full HP to damaged), a brief "aggro ping" fires: a small expanding ring in the enemy's color at the enemy's position (via the existing `spawn_spawn_ring` helper, scaled by enemy size) plus a quick emission energy brighten (2.5× → normal over 0.3s with ease-out quadratic). This gives the player a "I connected" feedback moment that also visually signals the enemy's transition from idle/wandering to active combat. Previously, the first hit on an enemy had the standard hit flash (white albedo + emission spike) + hit SFX + damage number, but no distinct "this enemy just woke up" visual — the alert indicator ("!") fires on detection (before the first hit), and the hit flash fires on every hit (not just the first). The aggro flash is a one-time event that bridges the gap — it fires exactly once per enemy, on the first damage, and communicates "this enemy is now in the fight." Uses the same `spawn_spawn_ring` visual language as the enemy spawn materialization ring for consistency. Skipped for enemies that spawn already damaged (HP < max_hp at first hit check).

## Enhancement Pack 29 — Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination + Biome Transition Ripple + 3D Positional Spawn SFX

### Wandering Merchant Spawner O(n) → O(1) Group Scan Elimination (`wandering_merchant_spawner.gd`)
- **Cached merchant count replaces per-frame group scan** — the spawner's `_process()` previously called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("wandering_merchant").size()` every single frame just to check if a merchant was alive (for the max-alive cap). This is an O(n) scene-tree group scan that runs 60 times per second even when no merchant exists and the spawner is just counting down the timer. Now a cached `_active_count` integer is incremented when a merchant spawns and decremented when one is removed (via the `tree.node_removed` signal, filtered by group membership). The per-frame check becomes a simple integer comparison (O(1)). The `_on_game_restarted()` handler resets the counter to 0 alongside the existing despawn loop. This eliminates a hidden per-frame allocation from the hot loop — small in isolation, but these per-frame group scans add up across the ~50 autoloads and their `_process` functions.

### Boss Tension Vignette Player Cache (`boss_tension_vignette.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-frame group lookup** — the boss tension vignette's `_process()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every frame to calculate the proximity-based pulse intensity. While `get_first_node_in_group` is cheaper than `get_nodes_in_group().size()` (it short-circuits on the first match), it still traverses the scene tree's group dictionary and does pointer comparisons every frame. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily via a `_get_player()` helper that only re-scans when the cache is stale (null or freed). This matches the caching pattern already used by `enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, and `wildlife.gd` — bringing the vignette in line with the codebase's established player-cache convention.

### Biome Transition Ground Ripple (`player.gd`)
- **Expanding ring + light column on biome change** — when the player crosses a biome boundary, an expanding ground ring now spawns at their feet colored by the new biome's fog color, accompanied by a brief 4m-tall upward light column that fades over 0.6s. This gives the biome transition a tangible physical "energy wave" presence that complements the existing audio crossfade (music drone swap), fog shader blend (per-biome color/density cross-fade), and biome indicator color lerp. Previously, biome transitions were primarily audio + fog + HUD label changes — the player's avatar had no direct feedback that they'd crossed into a new biome. The ring uses the existing `spawn_spawn_ring()` helper (shared with enemy spawn materialization) at 6m max radius with the ease-out cubic curve, and the light column uses a dimmer version of the ripple color (0.3× emission) so it reads as a "biome energy surge" rather than a beacon. The column is shorter than boss death beams (4m vs 12-30m) so it's a subtle environmental accent, not a spectacle. The ripple color is pulled from the `BIOME_FOG` dictionary for each biome — so entering the Deep Ocean produces a dark blue ripple, Volcano Core produces a dark red ripple, Sky Citadel produces a light blue ripple, etc. Each biome gets its own visual identity for the transition.

### 3D Positional Spawn Warning SFX (`audio_manager.gd` + `spawn_warning.gd`)
- **`play_sfx_3d()` API added to AudioManager** — a new method that plays a one-shot SFX as a 3D positional sound at a given world position using `AudioStreamPlayer3D`. Uses Godot's built-in inverse-distance attenuation model (natural rolloff to 60m max distance) with `unit_size=8.0` for readable gameplay audio. The player auto-frees after playback finishes via the `finished` signal. This is the first 3D positional audio API in the game — all previous SFX used non-positional `AudioStreamPlayer` which plays at a fixed volume regardless of the source's position in the world.
- **Spawn warning warp SFX now positional** — the spawn warning ring's anticipation flash "warp" sound (SFX_RIFT) previously used `play_sfx_volume()` with a manual smoothstep distance attenuation calculation. While the volume scaled correctly with distance, the sound was non-directional — the player heard "something is spawning nearby" but couldn't tell which direction it was coming from. Now it uses `play_sfx_3d()` at the spawn ring's world position, so Godot's 3D audio system handles both distance attenuation AND directional cueing (stereo panning based on the listener's position relative to the source). The player can now hear whether an enemy is materializing to their left, right, behind, or in front — critical for off-screen spawns where the visual telegraph isn't visible. This eliminates the manual distance calculation (simpler code) while adding directional information (richer audio).

## Enhancement Pack 30 — 3D Audio Attenuation Fix + Shared Material Allocation Elimination + Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination

### 3D Audio Attenuation Model Fix (`audio_manager.gd`)
- **ATTENUATION_LINEAR → ATTENUATION_INVERSE_DISTANCE** — the `play_sfx_3d()` API (Enhancement Pack 29) used `ATTENUATION_LINEAR` which produces an unnatural abrupt cutoff at `max_distance` (volume reaches exactly 0 at 60m, creating a hard "sound wall"). The inverse-distance attenuation model is the physically-based default for 3D game audio — it fades gradually, matching how real sound propagates through air. Sounds near the source are full volume, and they rolloff naturally with distance rather than hitting a hard zero. This is the standard model used in Unity, Unreal, and most game engines. The `unit_size=8.0` and `max_distance=60.0` parameters remain the same — only the attenuation curve shape changes, so the gameplay audio readability is preserved while the audio feels more natural.

### Poison Cloud Per-Tick Group Scan Elimination (`poison_cloud.gd`)
- **GameManager.enemies array replaces get_nodes_in_group("enemies")** — the poison cloud's damage tick (every 0.7s) used `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` to find enemies within the cloud radius. The `GameManager.enemies` array is already maintained (enemies are added on spawn, removed on death), so iterating it directly avoids a scene-tree group scan per tick. During heavy combat with multiple toxic spore deaths, several poison clouds can be ticking simultaneously — each one was doing its own group scan every 0.7s. Now they all iterate the pre-maintained array.
- **Cached P1 player reference** — the tick also called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every tick to check P1 proximity. Now a `_cached_p1` field is populated lazily and only re-scanned when the cache is stale (null or freed), matching the caching pattern used by `enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, and `wildlife.gd`.

### World Boss Manager Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`world_boss_manager.gd`)
- **Redundant per-frame get_nodes_in_group("enemies") removed** — the `_process()` function called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` every frame to count existing world bosses (safety check: only one at a time). But the `_active_world_boss` field already tracks the single active world boss — if `_active_world_boss` is null, there is no world boss. The per-frame group scan was completely redundant: it iterated all enemies in the scene just to count how many had `is_world_boss == true`, when the manager already knew the answer from its own tracking field. Removed the loop entirely; the spawn timer now advances directly when `_active_world_boss` is null. This eliminates an O(n) scene-tree group scan from the per-frame hot loop.

### Shared Tumble Visual Materials (`collectible.gd`)
- **Per-type shared StandardMaterial3D for tumble proxies** — the tumble RigidBody3D proxy (spawned on every collectible drop for the bounce-and-tumble animation) allocated a new `StandardMaterial3D` per drop with 6+ property assignments (albedo_color, shading_mode, emission_enabled, emission, rim_enabled, rim, rim_tint). The material only varies by the collectible type's color (from `TYPE_CONFIG`) and is never tweened at runtime — the proxy is a short-lived physics visual that gets hidden when the bounce settles. Now one material per collectible type is cached in a static dictionary (`_shared_tumble_mats`) and shared across all tumble proxies of that type. Since no per-instance property writes happen after creation (the proxy is hidden, not faded), sharing is safe. This eliminates per-drop `StandardMaterial3D` allocation during combat, where multiple enemies can die simultaneously and each drop spawns a tumble proxy. Mirrors the existing shared physics material pattern (`_shared_tumble_phys_mat`) and the shared mesh pattern (`_shared_meshes`).

### Shared Corpse Material Base (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **Duplicate shared base material instead of creating from scratch** — the physics corpse spawned on enemy death created a `StandardMaterial3D` from scratch per kill with 6+ property assignments (albedo_color, shading_mode, emission_enabled, emission, transparency). The only per-instance variation is the color (`base_color`) and the alpha fade tween — the shading mode, emission enable, and transparency settings are identical across all corpses. Now a single `_shared_corpse_mat_base` is created once with the pre-configured properties (SHADING_MODE_UNSHADED, emission_enabled, TRANSPARENCY_ALPHA), and each corpse calls `duplicate()` on it, then overwrites only `albedo_color` and `emission`. `duplicate()` is a single-shot copy of all pre-configured properties, which is measurably cheaper than 6+ individual property sets. The duplicate still gets its own `albedo_color.a` for the fade-out tween, which writes independently. This eliminates per-kill `StandardMaterial3D` allocation overhead during heavy combat where multiple enemies can die in quick succession.

### Spawn Warning Light Pooling (`spawn_warning.gd`)
- **PerformanceOptimizer transient light pool for spawn warning glow** — each spawn warning created a standalone `OmniLight3D` as a child node, then freed it when the warning ended. During heavy combat (swarm packs, endless mode, boss gauntlet), multiple spawn warnings can be active simultaneously — each creating + freeing a light. Now the warning acquires a light from the `PerformanceOptimizer` transient light pool via `acquire_transient_light()` instead of allocating a new one. The pool reuses dormant `OmniLight3D` nodes, eliminating per-warning light allocation churn. The pool auto-reclaims the light after `duration + 0.3s` (slightly longer than the warning duration so the pop tween's light fade has time to complete). All per-frame light manipulations (energy, range, color) and the pop tween's light fade are guarded with `is_instance_valid()` since the pool may reclaim the light if the timing is tight (edge case during scene teardown). A fallback path creates a standalone light if `PerformanceOptimizer` is not available (safety).

## Enhancement Pack 32 — Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (Graviton + Spitter + World Boss Manager) + Enrage Aura Shared Material

### Graviton Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_graviton.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the Graviton's `_physics_process()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every physics frame (60 times per second) to find the player for its gravity pull logic. This is an O(n) scene-tree group scan that ran continuously for every active Graviton. Now the Graviton uses the `_cached_player` field from the base `EnemyBase` class, which is populated by `_update_ai()` (called from `super._physics_process()`) and lazily re-scanned only when stale (null or freed). If the cache is empty or invalid, it falls back to a one-shot scan. This eliminates the per-frame group scan from every Graviton in the scene — during heavy combat with multiple Gravitons, each one was doing its own scene-tree traversal every frame. Matches the caching pattern already used by `enemy_base.gd`, `enemy_crystal_guardian.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, and `boss_tension_vignette.gd`.

### Spitter Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_spitter.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the Spore Spitter's `_update_ai()` override called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every physics frame. Since the Spitter overrides `_update_ai()` without calling `super._update_ai()`, the base class's `_cached_player` population logic never ran — the Spitter was always doing a fresh group scan every frame. Now the override populates `_cached_player` itself using the same lazy-scan pattern: check if the cache is valid, and only scan the scene tree when it's stale or empty. This eliminates the per-frame group scan from every active Spitter. The Spitter is a ranged kiter that stays alive longer than melee enemies (it keeps distance), so its per-frame scan cost was particularly persistent — each Spitter would scan the scene tree every frame for its entire lifetime.

### World Boss Manager Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`world_boss_manager.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the `_process()` function called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every frame when a world boss was active, for the despawn-on-flee distance check. World bosses can live for several minutes (they're tanky, roaming enemies), so this scan ran continuously for the entire duration of a world boss encounter — potentially hundreds of thousands of scene-tree traversals over a single world boss's lifetime. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily and only re-scanned when stale (null or freed). The cache is cleared on `game_restarted` since the player node is freed and re-created. Matches the caching pattern used across the codebase.
