The `_try_spawn_world_boss()` function still does a one-shot scan (it's called only when the spawn timer fires, not per-frame, so caching there would be premature).

### Enrage Aura Shared Base Material (`enemy_ai_controller.gd`)
- **Shared static base material for enrage auras** — the `_create_enrage_aura()` function created a `StandardMaterial3D` from scratch per enemy that entered enrage, with 6 property assignments (albedo_color, transparency, shading_mode, emission_enabled, emission, emission_energy_multiplier). All enrage auras use identical material properties — the only per-instance variation is the `albedo_color.a` pulse, which writes to the duplicate's own alpha independently. Now a static `_shared_enrage_mat_base` is created once with the pre-configured properties, and each aura calls `duplicate()` on it. `duplicate()` copies all pre-configured properties in a single call, which is cheaper than 6 individual property sets. The duplicate gets its own `albedo_color` (including alpha) so the per-frame pulse in `_update_enrage` doesn't affect other auras. Mirrors the shared-base-material pattern already used by `enemy_base.gd` (`_shared_corpse_mat_base`) and `collectible.gd` (`_shared_tumble_mats`). During heavy combat with multiple enemies entering enrage simultaneously (e.g. a pack of enemies all dropping below 25% HP from an AoE), each one was allocating a new material — now they all share a single base.

## Enhancement Pack 34 — Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (Shockwave + Minimap + Sentinel + Ancient Sentinel + Gravity Elemental) + Enemy Spawner Array Optimization + Void Wisp Teleport Feedback

### Shockwave Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`shockwave.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the shockwave ring's `_physics_process()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every frame while expanding (~0.5s per shot). During heavy combat with multiple Sentinels firing shockwaves simultaneously, each ring was doing its own scene-tree group scan every frame. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily and only re-scanned when stale (null or freed). Matches the caching pattern used by `enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, `boss_tension_vignette.gd`, etc.

### Minimap Per-Draw Group Scan Elimination (`minimap.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-draw `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the minimap's `_draw_terrain()` and `_draw_entity_dots()` both called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` on every `_draw()` call. With `MINIMAP_DOT_REFRESH_INTERVAL=0.05s`, this produced ~40 scene-tree group scans per second (20 per draw × 2 calls per draw). Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily via a `_get_player()` helper and only re-scanned when stale. The minimap is part of the HUD scene which is freed and re-created on scene reload, so the cache naturally resets between runs.

### Starburst Sentinel Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_sentinel.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the Sentinel's `_update_ai()` override called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every physics frame without calling `super._update_ai()`, so the base class `_cached_player` was never populated. The Sentinel is a stationary turret that stays alive for a long time, making the per-frame scan particularly persistent. Now uses the same lazy-scan pattern as `enemy_spitter.gd` (Enhancement Pack 32). The fallback direct-damage path also uses `_cached_player`.

### Ancient Sentinel Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_ancient_sentinel.gd`)
- **Cached player in `_spawn_falling_pillar()` and `_apply_aoe_damage()`** — both methods called `get_first_node_in_group("player")` on each invocation. The Ancient Sentinel overrides `_update_ai` to `pass` (stationary boss), so the base class cache was never populated. Now both methods use the lazy-scan pattern to populate `_cached_player` from the base class. `_spawn_falling_pillar` fires up to 8 times per pillar phase, and `_apply_aoe_damage` fires on every nova ring tick — eliminating these scans reduces the boss's per-phase group scans from dozens to one.

### Gravity Elemental Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_gravity_elemental.gd`)
- **`_repel_player()` and `_fling_loose_objects()` now use base class `_cached_player`** — both methods called `get_first_node_in_group("player")` on each invocation. The Gravity Elemental calls `super._physics_process()`, so the base class `_cached_player` IS populated — but these methods weren't using it. Now they use the cached reference with a lazy fallback scan, eliminating per-tick group scans during the gravity field's active phase (1.5s duration, ticking every 0.2s = 7-8 scans per field activation eliminated).

### Enemy Spawner Array Optimization (`enemy_spawner.gd`)
- **`get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` replaced with `GameManager.enemies` array** — the `_try_spawn()` function called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` on every spawn tick to count active enemies and check nearby density. The `GameManager.enemies` array is already maintained (append on materialize, erase on death) and is the canonical enemy list. Iterating it directly avoids a scene-tree group scan per spawn tick. Matches the optimization pattern from `poison_cloud.gd` (Enhancement Pack 30) and `pulse_wave.gd` (Enhancement Pack 31).

### Void Wisp Teleport Particle Bursts + SFX (`enemy_wisp.gd`)
- **Departure + arrival particle bursts** — the Void Wisp's teleport (which repositions it behind the player) previously had only a material fade — no particle effects. Now a small 8-particle burst spawns at both the departure position and the arrival position, giving the player a visual cue of where the Wisp came from and where it reappeared. This is critical for combat awareness since the Wisp teleports behind the player — without the arrival burst, the player had no idea where the Wisp reappeared unless they were looking directly at it.
- **Teleport SFX** — a subtle `SFX_RIFT` whoosh at 0.4× volume plays on teleport, giving the player an audio cue that the Wisp has repositioned. Previously the teleport was completely silent — the player could be ambushed from behind with no warning. The low volume (0.4×) ensures it doesn't compete with combat sounds while still being audible.

## Enhancement Pack 35 — Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (Bomber + Drake + Arena Hazard + Ambient Particles) + Bomber Explosion Light Pooling

### Bomber Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_bomber.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the Bomber's `_physics_process()` fuse trigger check now uses `_cached_player` from the base class (populated by `super._physics_process`) instead of `get_first_node_in_group("player")` every physics frame. The Bomber is a kamikaze that runs toward the player for its entire lifetime, so this scan ran continuously from detection until fuse trigger.
- **Bomber explosion uses `GameManager.enemies` array** — `_explode()` now iterates `GameManager.enemies` instead of `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` for the friendly-fire AoE damage, matching the optimization pattern from `poison_cloud.gd`, `pulse_wave.gd`, and `shockwave.gd`.
- **Bomber explosion light pooling** — `_explode()` now uses `PerformanceOptimizer.acquire_transient_light()` instead of allocating a new `OmniLight3D` per explosion, matching the shared light pool pattern used by `enemy_base.gd` death light, `projectile.gd` flash/shrapnel lights, `spawn_warning.gd` glow light, `collectible.gd` pickup light, and `destructible.gd` shatter light, with a non-pooled fallback path.

### Drake Boss Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_drake.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-physics-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — `_update_boss_attacks()` now uses `_cached_player` from the base class instead of `get_first_node_in_group("player")` every physics frame. The Drake is a boss that lives for an extended fight, making the per-frame scan particularly persistent.

### Arena Hazard Group Scan Elimination (`arena_hazard.gd`)
- **`GameManager.enemies` array replaces `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")`** — `_deal_damage_in_radius()` now iterates `GameManager.enemies` array instead of `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` for the enemy AoE damage. Arena hazards (lava geysers, falling crystals, void shockwaves) deal damage on activation, and void shockwaves deal continuous damage during the active phase, so this scan ran on every hazard activation and every frame during void shockwave expansion.

### Ambient Particles Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`ambient_particles.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces periodic `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — `_process()` now uses a cached `_cached_player` field with lazy re-scan instead of `get_first_node_in_group("player")` every 0.5s interval. The particle node follows the player for the entire game session, so this scan ran twice per second continuously from game start to end.

## Enhancement Pack 36 — Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (Spawn Direction Indicator + Swarm Queen + Void Leviathan + Endgame Loot Cave)

### Spawn Direction Indicator Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`spawn_direction_indicator.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces per-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")`** — the `_process()` function called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every frame to get the player's position for the off-screen enemy arrow calculations. The spawn direction indicator runs continuously from game start to end (it listens to `enemy_spawned_near` and fades arrows over time), so this scan ran every frame even when no arrows are active. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily via a `_get_player()` helper and only re-scans when stale (null or freed). Matches the caching pattern used by `enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, `minimap.gd`, `boss_tension_vignette.gd`, and `ambient_particles.gd`.

### Swarm Queen Enemy Cap Check Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_swarm_queen.gd`)
- **`GameManager.enemies` array replaces `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")`** — `_spawn_mite_batch()` called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` on every batch spawn attempt (every 3-5s) to check the global enemy cap before spawning new mites. The `GameManager.enemies` array is already maintained (append on materialize, erase on death) and is the canonical enemy list. Iterating it directly avoids a scene-tree group scan per batch attempt. Matches the optimization pattern from `poison_cloud.gd`, `pulse_wave.gd`, `enemy_bomber.gd`, and `arena_hazard.gd`.

### Void Leviathan Summon Cap Check Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_void_leviathan.gd`)
- **`GameManager.enemies` array replaces `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")`** — `_summon_wisps()` called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` on every summon attempt (every 8s during stage 2+) to check the global enemy cap before summoning Void Wisps. Now iterates `GameManager.enemies` directly, matching the same optimization pattern. The Void Leviathan is a boss with a long fight, so these scans accumulate over the encounter duration.

### Endgame Loot Cave Clear Check Group Scan Elimination (`endgame_manager.gd`)
- **Endgame Loot Cave Clear Check Group Scan Elimination (`endgame_manager.gd`)** — `_check_loot_cave_clear()` called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` to count remaining enemies near each undiscovered/unclaered loot cave. This function is called on every `boss_defeated` signal, which can fire frequently during loot cave runs with multiple elite guards. Now iterates `GameManager.enemies` directly, matching the same optimization pattern.

## Enhancement Pack 38 — Minimap Per-Draw Group Scan Elimination
- **Cached group arrays replace 12 per-draw `get_nodes_in_group()` calls** — the minimap's `_draw_entity_dots()` called `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group()` for 12 different groups on every `_draw()` call: portals, rifts, trader, lore_stone, treasure_chest, wildlife, dialogue_npc, env_hazard, interactive_object, fast_travel_waypoint, ping, and companion_pet. With the minimap redrawing at ~20 FPS (`MINIMAP_DOT_REFRESH_INTERVAL=0.05s`), this produced **~240 scene-tree group scans per second** (12 groups × 20 draws/sec). Each `get_nodes_in_group()` call traverses the scene tree's group dictionary, which is O(members) per call — during a busy session with portals, rifts, wildlife, NPCs, hazards, chests, waypoints, pings, and pets all active, the combined cost was significant.
- **Periodic group cache refresh** — a new `_group_cache` dictionary stores arrays of nodes for each of the 12 groups, refreshed every `GROUP_CACHE_REFRESH_INTERVAL` (0.1s = 10×/sec) in `_process()`. This is 2× slower than the draw rate, so each refresh covers ~2 draws — the group membership changes at most 10×/sec instead of 240×/sec. The total scene-tree group scan rate drops from ~240/sec to ~120/sec (12 groups × 10 refreshes/sec), a **50% reduction**. The nodes are still validated with `is_instance_valid()` during iteration (they may be freed between refreshes), so stale entries are harmlessly skipped — the same pattern used by the `GameManager.enemies` array.
- **`_get_cached_group()` helper** — returns the cached array for a group name, with a fallback to a direct `get_nodes_in_group()` call on the first draw (before the first refresh tick populates the cache). This ensures the first frame after the minimap becomes visible doesn't have a blank minimap.
- **`CACHED_GROUPS` constant** — the 12 group names are declared as a `const Array[String]` so the refresh loop iterates a known list rather than dynamically discovering groups. This makes the caching explicit and prevents accidental omissions if new minimap dot types are added in the future.
- **Impact** — this is the highest-frequency remaining group scan hotspot in the codebase. All previous enhancement packs (29-36) eliminated per-frame `get_first_node_in_group("player")` and `get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` calls from individual scripts, but the minimap's 12 per-draw calls were the largest concentration of group scans in a single function. During a typical session with the minimap visible for the entire game, this eliminates millions of unnecessary scene-tree traversals over a long play session. The minimap is the most frequently redrawn UI element (20 FPS vs the HUD's 60 FPS `_process` but with heavier per-draw work due to tile rendering + 12 entity group iterations).

## Enhancement Pack 39 — Time Warden Field Feedback + Gravity Charge SFX + Echo Knight Phantom Spawn Feedback

### Time Warden Slow Field Entry/Exit Player Feedback (`player.gd` + `player2_zerp.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_TIME_SLOW_ENTER** — a soft descending chime (G5→E5→C5, 784→659→523 Hz, 0.10s, 0.12 vol) that plays when the player enters a Time Warden's slowing field. The gentle descending interval conveys "time is bending" — a subtle temporal warp cue. Very quiet so it doesn't compete with combat sounds.
- **Entry/exit transition detection** — previously the player silently slowed down when entering a Time Warden's slowing field with no audio or visual cue at all. The `EnemyTimeWarden.get_player_slow_mult()` was queried every physics frame but the result was only used as a speed multiplier — the transition from "not in field" to "in field" was invisible. Now `player.gd` and `player2_zerp.gd` track `_in_time_warden_field` and fire the SFX on the enter transition (descending chime at 1.0× pitch) and on the exit transition (same chime at 1.3× pitch — higher, conveying "time normalizes"). The threshold (0.99× speed multiplier) handles the smooth field edge falloff cleanly.
- **Brief blue emission tint on entry** — when P1 enters the field, the player material's emission briefly shifts to a cool blue (Color(0.2, 0.4, 0.8), 2.5× energy) then eases back to the original emission over 0.4s (ease-out). This gives the player an on-character visual cue that they've entered a temporal slow zone — the blue tint reads as "cold/time is thick here." The tween captures the original emission color and energy so it restores cleanly regardless of what other emission systems (idle breathing, dash exhaustion, heartbeat) were active.
- **P2 co-op parity** — `player2_zerp.gd` gets the same `_in_time_warden_field` tracking and SFX enter/exit cues (without the emission tint since P2's material handling is simpler). Both players get the audio feedback independently.
- **Reset on game restart** — `_in_time_warden_field` is reset to `false` in `_on_game_restarted_player()` so stale field state doesn't carry into the next run.

### Gravity Elemental Charging Dedicated SFX (`enemy_gravity_elemental.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_GRAVITY_CHARGE** — a deep descending rumble (70→35 Hz over 0.50s, 0.35 vol) that plays when a Gravity Elemental begins charging its repel field. The very low frequency sweep conveys massive gravitational energy building up — the sound of space warping.
- **Replaces SFX_ARENA** — the Gravity Elemental's `_start_charging()` previously used `SFX_ARENA` (a generic 50 Hz arena hazard rumble) which was shared with boss arena construction and didn't convey the unique gravitic nature of the attack. The arena rumble is a generic "something big is happening" sound; the new `SFX_GRAVITY_CHARGE` is a deeper, longer, descending sweep that specifically reads as "gravity is warping" — matching the visual of the growing translucent sphere + brightening emission during the 0.8s charge telegraph. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning.

### Echo Knight Shadow Copy Spawn & Dissolve Feedback (`enemy_echo_knight.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_PHANTOM_SPAWN** — an ethereal shimmering chime (B4→D5→F#5, 494→587→740 Hz, 0.18s, 0.18 vol) that plays when an Echo Knight summons its shadow copies at spawn. The augmented triad (B-D-F#) has an unsettled, "otherworldly" quality that conveys phantoms splitting from a real body — distinct from any other SFX in the game. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning.
- **Per-copy spawn particle bursts** — each shadow copy now spawns a small 10-particle burst in the Echo Knight's color at its offset position when created, so the player sees the phantoms materialize rather than pop in. Previously the copies appeared with no visual feedback at all — they just silently appeared as translucent spheres at their fixed offsets.
- **Per-copy dissolve particle bursts on death** — when the Echo Knight dies, each shadow copy now gets a small 6-particle dissolve burst at its offset position before the existing fade-out tween. Previously the copies just faded out — the player saw them dissolve but had no particle feedback for the "phantom shattering" moment. Combined with the existing 20-particle death burst on the real knight, the death now has a cascading visual from real body → copies dissolving one by one.

## Enhancement Pack 40 — Enemy Projectile Particle Trail + Pulse Wave Hit Spark + Void Wisp Teleport Cache

### Enemy Projectile Particle Trail (`enemy_projectile.gd`)
- **GPUParticles3D exhaust trail on enemy bolts** — enemy projectiles (Spore Spitter spit, Plasma Drake fireballs, Crystal Guardian shards, Mirror Mimic bolts, Void Leviathan void breath, Shadow Clone dark bolts, procedural boss projectile fans) now emit a short spark trail as they fly, making their trajectory readable even in dark biomes (Underground, Eclipse, Digital Grid) where the point light alone isn't enough to track the bolt's path. Player projectiles already had a 6-point mesh trail; enemy bolts had only a point light + velocity-aligned stretch + rifled spin but no trail, making them harder to see in darkness — the player could hear the graze whoosh but couldn't always see where the bolt came from. The trail uses 12 particles with a 0.25s lifetime in world-space coordinates (`local_coords = false`) so particles stay where emitted, creating a proper "exhaust stream" that marks the bolt's path through the air. The trail's color matches each projectile's own `projectile_color` via a duplicated `ParticleProcessMaterial` (from a shared base) with a gradient fade ramp — so Spore Spitter spit leaves an orange trail, Crystal Guardian shards leave a crystal-blue trail, Drake fireballs leave a fiery red trail, etc. The shared `SphereMesh` draw pass (0.06m radius, 4 radial segments — minimal geometry) is reused across all trails. Trail emission stops on hit, terrain collision, and lifetime fizzle so the stream ends cleanly with the bolt's dissipation rather than lingering. Performance: 12 particles × 0.25s lifetime = ~3 active particles per bolt at any time, negligible vs. the existing 500-particle weather systems. Shared resources (`_shared_trail_process_mat`, `_shared_trail_mesh`) are created once and reused, mirroring the shared-resource pattern used by the bolt's own material and mesh.

### Pulse Wave Hit Spark on Each Enemy (`pulse_wave.gd`)
- **Cyan spark burst at each enemy the wave reaches** — when the player's Q ability pulse wave expands and hits an enemy, a small 6-particle cyan spark burst now spawns at that enemy's position, so the player sees the shockwave "punching" each enemy it reaches. Without this, the wave expanded silently through enemies — the damage numbers + the enemy's own hit flash were the only feedback, which could be hard to read when multiple enemies were hit simultaneously (the wave can sweep through 5+ enemies in one cast). The spark gives each hit a distinct physical "impact" moment that traces the wave's expanding front, making the AoE feel like a real shockwave hitting each enemy in sequence rather than an invisible damage zone. The spark uses the pulse wave's cyan color (Color(0.2, 1.0, 0.8)), matching the ring visual, and reuses the existing `ParticleEffects.spawn_explosion()` helper (6 particles, 0.15s duration — small and quick). The enhancement also applies to the co-op mega pulse wave since that spawns additional `PulseWave` instances that share the same hit-detection code. The spark fires once per enemy per wave (gated by the existing `has_hit` dictionary that prevents double-damage), so each enemy gets exactly one spark.

### Void Wisp Teleport Cached Player (`enemy_wisp.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces group scan on teleport** — the Void Wisp's `_teleport_behind_player()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` on every teleport. While this is an event handler (not per-frame), the Wisp calls `super._physics_process()` every frame, which populates the base class `_cached_player` field via `_update_ai()`. Now the teleport uses `_cached_player` first, falling back to a one-shot group scan only when the cache is stale (null or freed) — matching the caching pattern used across the codebase (`enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, `minimap.gd`, etc.). The Wisp teleports frequently (50% chance on every hit, with a 1.5s cooldown), so during combat with multiple Wisps this eliminates several group scans per second. This also matches the Enhancement Pack 34 fix for the Void Wisp's sibling enemies (Sentinel, Ancient Sentinel, Gravity Elemental) that were already fixed but the Wisp's teleport path was missed.

## Enhancement Pack 41 — Deployable System Audio-Visual Feedback Polish

### Shield Bubble Damage Absorb SFX + Visual Pulse (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_SHIELD_HIT** — a crisp metallic "ting" (1200 Hz, 0.06s, 0.18 vol) that plays when the Shield Bubble absorbs incoming damage. The high metallic frequency conveys a hard energy shield deflecting a hit. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning. Previously the shield bubble absorbing damage had only the HUD HP bar shrinking — no audio cue at all that the shield had blocked a hit. The player had to watch the shield HP bar to know their bubble was taking damage, which was easy to miss during combat.
- **Emission pulse on absorption** — the bubble mesh's `emission_energy_multiplier` flashes to 5.0 then eases back over 0.20s (ease-out) each time damage is absorbed, giving the bubble a visible "impact flicker" that reads as the shield actively deflecting energy. Previously the bubble pulsed on a fixed 0.5s loop but had no reactive visual when actually absorbing a hit — the player saw no difference between a shield taking damage and a shield sitting idle.

### Shield Bubble Break SFX (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_SHIELD_BREAK** — a shattering descending arpeggio (C6→G5→C5→G4, 0.05s per note, 0.30 vol) that plays when the Shield Bubble's HP is depleted. The cascading downward arpeggio conveys "the shield has shattered" — a protective barrier collapsing. Distinct from SFX_SHIELD (warm ascending deploy chime) so the player distinguishes "shield activated" from "shield destroyed." Previously the shield breaking from damage had only the existing shatter particles (40 shard fragments) but no audio — the bubble vanished silently. The shatter particles were a purely visual event that was easy to miss in the heat of combat, especially in bright biomes where the blue shards blended with the environment.

### Shield Bubble Reflect SFX (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_SHIELD_REFLECT** — a quick ricochet blip (1800 Hz, 0.04s, 0.15 vol) that plays when the Shield Bubble reflects an enemy projectile back at the shooter. The very high pitch conveys a deflection — something bouncing off a hard surface. Quieter than the deploy/break sounds since reflects can happen in rapid succession during projectile-heavy encounters (Spore Spitters, Crystal Guardians, Mirror Mimics). Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning. Previously the reflection had a small particle burst (10 particles in the shield color) but no audio — the player saw the projectile reverse direction but heard nothing, making the reflection feel like a visual glitch rather than an intentional mechanic.

### Turret Deploy Fire + Hit + Destroyed + Expired SFX (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **Turret deploy SFX upgrade** — the turret deploy activation now plays `SFX_SHOOT_UTILITY` (soft deploy chime) instead of `SFX_SHOOT` (generic laser blip). The deploy is a deployment event, not a weapon fire — the utility chime conveys "equipment deployed" rather than "gun fired." Matches the shield bubble deploy which already used `SFX_SHIELD`.
- **Turret fire SFX** — each turret shot now plays `SFX_SHOOT_ENERGY` at 0.5× volume instead of `SFX_SHOOT` at full volume. The energy bolt sound is more distinct from the player's own weapon fire, and the reduced volume prevents the turret's rapid fire (every 0.35s) from drowning out the player's combat audio. Previously the turret firing was indistinguishable from the player's own shots by ear.
- **Turret bolt hit SFX** — when a turret bolt hits an enemy, `SFX_ENEMY_HIT` plays at 0.6× volume. Previously turret bolt hits had particles + damage numbers but no audio — the player saw the hit but heard nothing from the turret's contribution to combat.
- **New SFX_TURRET_DESTROYED** — a metallic crunch (descending 300→150 Hz, 0.20s, 0.35 vol) that plays when the turret is destroyed by enemy fire (HP depleted). The low descending tone conveys mechanical failure — gears grinding to a halt. Previously the turret being destroyed had only the shrink + fade + death poof particles but no audio — the player's deployed turret vanished silently when enemies destroyed it.
- **New SFX_TURRET_EXPIRED** — a gentle powering-down blip (600→300 Hz, 0.12s, 0.18 vol) that plays when the turret's 15-second duration runs out naturally. Quieter and shorter than the destroyed sound since natural expiration is less dramatic than being destroyed. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX`. Previously the turret expiring had only the shrink + fade + death poof particles but no audio — the player's turret silently disappeared when its timer ran out, with no indication of whether it was destroyed or just expired.
- **Turret takes damage SFX** — when the turret takes damage but survives (HP > 0), `SFX_ENEMY_HIT` plays at 0.5× volume. Previously enemies attacking the turret had no audio feedback — the player saw the emission flash but heard nothing, making it hard to notice that the turret was under attack.

### Gravity Flip Field Launch + Fall Damage SFX (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_GRAVITY_LAUNCH** — an upward whoosh (0.30s, 0.30 vol) that plays once when the Gravity Flip Field first launches enemies into the air. The longer duration (vs SFX_DASH's 0.18s) conveys a sustained upward force. Previously the gravity flip field launching enemies had only purple particle trails but no audio — enemies silently floated upward with no sonic cue of the gravitational force being applied. The SFX fires only once (tracked via `_launch_sfx_played` flag) rather than on every tick, preventing audio spam during the 4-second field duration.
- **Fall damage impact SFX** — when launched enemies land and take fall damage, `SFX_LAND` plays at 0.6× volume for each enemy that takes fall damage. Previously the fall damage had particles + damage numbers but no audio — enemies silently slammed back to the ground. The landing thud conveys the physical impact of gravity reasserting itself.

### Void Rift Cutter Slash SFX (`deployable_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_VOID_SLASH** — a sharp ethereal slash (descending 880→440 Hz, 0.10s, 0.18 vol) that plays when an enemy passes through the Void Rift Cutter and takes damage. The descending tone conveys a blade cutting through space — a void blade slicing reality. Quiet since multiple enemies can pass through in quick succession. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning. Previously enemies passing through the rift had particles + damage numbers but no audio — the rift silently damaged enemies with no sonic cue of the void blade's cutting effect. The slash sound gives each hit a distinct "blade slice" moment that makes the rift feel like an active weapon rather than a passive damage zone.

### Shield Bubble Cached Player (`deployable_system.gd`)
- **Cached player reference replaces group scans** — the `_on_shield_bubble_body_entered()` and `_break_shield_bubble()` functions called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` on each invocation. While these are event handlers (not per-frame), the shield bubble can reflect multiple projectiles in rapid succession during projectile-heavy encounters, and the break event happens at a critical combat moment. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily via a `_get_player()` helper and only re-scanned when stale (null or freed), matching the caching pattern used by `enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, `minimap.gd`, etc. The cache is cleared on `game_restarted` since the player node is freed and re-created.

## Enhancement Pack 42 — Universal Tween Easing Curve Completion

### TRANS_QUAD Added to All 110 Linear-Default Tweens (39 files)
- **All tweens with .set_ease() now have .set_trans()** — 110 tweens across 39 GDScript files had `.set_ease(Tween.EASE_OUT/IN/IN_OUT)` but no `.set_trans()` call, meaning they defaulted to `TRANS_LINEAR` (linear interpolation). While the easing direction was specified, the underlying curve was linear — making the "ease" merely a linear ramp rather than a natural curve. Adding `.set_trans(Tween.TRANS_QUAD)` gives every tween a quadratic curve that produces a natural deceleration (EASE_OUT), acceleration (EASE_IN), or S-curve (EASE_IN_OUT) instead of a constant-rate change. This is the standard game-feel improvement — quadratic easing is the most common transition curve in game UI/animation because it reads as natural motion without being as dramatic as cubic or elastic.
- **Files affected** (top 10 by fix count): `settings_menu.gd` (10), `deployable_system.gd` (9), `particle_effects.gd` (7), `collectible.gd` (5), `player.gd` (4), `pause_menu.gd` (4), `main_menu.gd` (4), `intro_cinematic.gd` (4), `enemy_base.gd` (4), `destructible.gd` (4), plus 29 more files with 1-3 fixes each.
- **Impact** — every visual tween in the game now has a proper nonlinear curve: light energy fades decelerate naturally instead of dimming at a constant rate, scale animations ease in/out instead of growing/shrinking linearly, alpha transitions have the familiar quadratic feel, UI panel movements decelerate as they arrive, and particle scale changes ease naturally. This is a subtle but pervasive improvement to the game's entire animation feel — the kind of polish that players notice subconsciously ("the game feels smoother") without being able to identify why.

## Enhancement Pack 44 — AI State Transition SFX & Visual Feedback + Weather Combo Dedicated Audio

### AI State Transition Audio Cues (`enemy_ai_controller.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_AMBUSH_TRIGGER** — a sharp ascending blip (600→1200 Hz, 0.06s, 0.12 vol) that plays when an ambush enemy breaks cover and rushes the player. The fast upward sweep conveys ambush — a concealed threat springing. Previously the ambush break had only a visual "!" indicator — no audio at all. The player had no cue that a hidden enemy had sprung its trap unless they were looking directly at the "!" pop-in.
- **New SFX_PACK_FRENZY** — a dissonant descending cluster (440→220→110 Hz, 0.20s, 0.20 vol) that plays when a pack member's frenzy triggers nearby allies. The layered descending tones convey a group suddenly going berserk — distinctly different from the single-enemy enrage growl (SFX_ENRAGE) so the player can tell by ear whether one enemy or a whole pack just went frenzy. Previously pack frenzy had only a HUD message + orange particle burst, both of which could be missed in busy combat.
- **New SFX_CALL_HELP** — a rapid urgent double-blip (880 Hz, 0.04s, 0.10 vol) that plays when a wounded enemy calls for help and alerts nearby allies. The staccato pulse reads as "distress signal." Quiet since it's informational, not dramatic — the player should notice allies being alerted, not be startled. Previously call-for-help had only a HUD message + yellow particle burst, both easy to miss during intense combat.
- **New SFX_RETREAT** — a whimpering descending whine (330→165 Hz, 0.18s, 0.08 vol) that plays when an enemy starts retreating at low HP. The descending pitch conveys "deflating" — the opposite of enrage. Very quiet since retreat is a subtle tactical shift. Only fires on the transition (not while retreating), so no spam. Previously retreat had no audio feedback at all — the player had to notice the enemy backing away visually, which was easy to miss.
- All 4 SFX added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning (±6%).

### Ambush Break Emission Flash (`enemy_ai_controller.gd`)
- **Brief emission spike on ambush break** — when an ambush enemy breaks cover and rushes, the enemy's `emission_energy_multiplier` spikes to 6.0 (0.05s ramp) then eases back to the original value over 0.25s (TRANS_QUAD ease-out). This gives the player a visual cue at the enemy's position even if they weren't looking at the "!" indicator — the enemy briefly glows bright, drawing the eye to the ambush point. Previously the ambush break was visual-only via the "!" text indicator, which was easy to miss in busy combat.

### Retreat Start Visual Cue (`enemy_ai_controller.gd`)
- **Brief emission dim on retreat start** — when an enemy starts retreating, the enemy's `emission_energy_multiplier` drops to 0.3 (0.15s ramp) then eases back over 0.40s (TRANS_QUAD ease-out). The dimming conveys "this enemy is losing its nerve" — the opposite of the enrage emission boost. The longer restore time (0.40s vs 0.25s for ambush) reflects that retreat is a sustained state change, not a sudden event. Previously retreat had no visual feedback — the enemy just started moving away, which was easy to miss in the chaos of combat.

### Weather Combo Dedicated SFX (`weather_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_WEATHER_COMBO** — a shimmering 5-note ascending augmented arpeggio (C5→E5→G5→B5→E6, 0.05s per note, 0.22 vol) that plays when a weather combo activates. The wider 5-note range and augmented triad (B5 = augmented fifth) gives it an "overlapping / unusual" quality distinct from any single weather sound. Previously the weather combo reused SFX_COMBO_MILESTONE (the kill-streak sound), which was thematically misleading — the player would hear "kill streak" when a weather combo started, not "atmospheric event." The dedicated SFX reads as "something unusual is happening in the sky" rather than "combat reward." Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning.

## Enhancement Pack 46 — Final Linear-Default Tween Easing Completion

### Boss Enrage Color Transition Easing (`enemy_drake.gd` + `enemy_void_leviathan.gd` + `enemy_ancient_sentinel.gd`)
- **EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on all 3 boss enrage color tweens** — the Drake, Void Leviathan, and Ancient Sentinel all had enrage color transition tweens that defaulted to TRANS_LINEAR (linear interpolation). A boss entering its enrage phase is a dramatic moment — the color shift from the normal palette to the enraged red/purple should feel like a building surge, not a constant-rate gradient. EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_QUAD produces an S-curve that starts gently, accelerates through the middle, and settles at the end — reading as a "power building up then locking in" rather than a mechanical linear blend. The Drake's 0.5s transition, Void Leviathan's 0.6s, and Ancient Sentinel's 0.6s all now share this consistent enrage curve language.

### Telegraph Pulse Easing (`enemy_ancient_sentinel.gd` + `projectile.gd`)
- **EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE on telegraph pulse loops** — the Ancient Sentinel's pillar barrage telegraph (4× pulse between 0.5 and 2.0 emission) and the Meteor Strike's ground telegraph (4× pulse between 0.5 and 2.5 emission) both pulsed with linear interpolation between brightness levels. Linear pulsing reads as mechanical on/off switching — like a fluorescent light flickering. EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE produces a smooth sinusoidal pulse that reads as organic energy buildup and release — the telegraph "breathes" rather than "clicks," making the warning feel like charging energy rather than a digital timer. The `set_ease()`/`set_trans()` is set on the Tween itself (not per-tween_property) so all 4 pulse cycles in the loop inherit the easing automatically.

### Companion Pet Emote & Hit Flash Easing (`companion_pet.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on emote emission flash** — the pet's emote system flashes emission to 3.0× then eases back over 0.3s. Previously linear — the flash dimmed at a constant rate, feeling like a light bulb turning off. EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD gives it a natural deceleration: a bright pop that gently fades, matching the emote label's own TRANS_BACK/ELASTIC pop-in. Now the visual flash and the label scale animation share the same "pop and settle" feel.
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on hit flash emission + color restore** — the pet's hit reaction (emission to 4.0× + white albedo flash) restored over 0.2s with linear interpolation. Now both the emission and albedo color restore use EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD, matching the enemy hit flash pattern in `enemy_base.gd`. The pet's hit reaction now has the same natural deceleration as enemy hits, creating visual consistency across all combat participants.

### Deployable System Fade & Flash Easing (`deployable_system.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on turret hit flash** — the turret's damage absorption flash (emission to 4.0× → 1.0× over 0.15s) was linear. Now uses EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD for a natural deceleration matching the Shield Bubble's own hit flash pattern.
- **EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on Void Rift Cutter fade-out** — the rift's 0.4s fade-out (albedo alpha, emission, and light energy) was linear. EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD gives the fade a gradual start that accelerates — the rift "dissolves" rather than uniformly dimming, matching how real energy dissipates.
- **EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on Gravity Flip Field fade-out** — the field's 0.3s albedo alpha fade was linear. Now matches the field light's existing EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD fade, creating a coherent "field collapsing" feel where the visual and light fade in sync.

### Treasure Chest Trap Flash Easing (`treasure_chest.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on trap flash restore** — the chest trap's red flash telegraph (albedo, emission, and emission_energy_multiplier restoring over 0.25s) was linear. EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD gives the flash a natural recovery — the harsh red snap eases back to normal with a decelerating settle, reading as "the trap's energy dissipating" rather than a mechanical color blend.

### Enemy Wisp Teleport Fade-In Easing (`enemy_wisp.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on teleport materialize alpha** — the Wisp's 0.3s alpha fade-in after teleporting was linear. EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD gives the materialization a "forming from nothing" feel — the Wisp starts faint and accelerates to full opacity, like a ghost gaining substance. Matches the enemy spawn materialization pattern (TRANS_BACK/EASE_OUT) in `enemy_base.gd`.

### Enemy Bomber Death Scale & Alpha Easing (`enemy_bomber.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on explosion scale-up + EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on alpha fade** — the Bomber's death explosion (scale to 3.0× over 0.15s + alpha to 0 over 0.15s) was linear. The scale-up now decelerates (EASE_OUT) — the explosion expands fast then slows, like a real blast losing energy. The alpha fade accelerates (EASE_IN) — the Bomber starts solid and dissolves faster as it disappears. Together they create a "blowing apart" feel rather than a uniform expand-and-fade.

### Pack Frenzy Flash Easing (`enemy_ai_controller.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on ally frenzy color flash** — when a pack frenzy triggers and allies flash to a bright color then restore, the restore was linear. EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD gives the flash a natural recovery matching the enemy hit flash pattern, so the frenzy flash reads as "a burst of energy that settles" rather than a mechanical color blend.

### Enemy Hit Flash Albedo Easing (`enemy_base.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on hit flash albedo restore** — the enemy hit flash already had EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on the emission restore, but the albedo color restore (flashing white → current_color over 0.15s) was linear. Now both the color and emission share the same easing, so the hit flash's visual and brightness components recover in sync — the white flash dims and the emission fades with the same deceleration curve, creating a unified "impact recovery" rather than two independent linear blends.

### Ping System Fade Easing (`ping_system.gd`)
- **EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on beam, ring, and light fade** — the ping beacon's 3 parallel fade-out tweens (beam alpha, ring alpha, light energy) were linear while the Label3D modulate fade already had EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD. Now all 4 components share the same easing, so the entire beacon dissolves as a cohesive unit rather than the label fading with a curve while the visual elements fade linearly.

### Weather System Light Fade Easing (`weather_system.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on thunder + meteor light fade parallel tweens** — the thunder strike's flash light_energy and omni_range fades (0.4s) and the meteor strike's flash light_energy and omni_range fades (0.6s) had the light_energy tween with `set_trans(TRANS_QUAD)` but no `set_ease()` (defaulting to EASE_IN), while the parallel omni_range tween was fully linear. Now both use EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD, so the light dims with a natural deceleration (fast initial flash that gently trails off) and the range contracts with the same curve — the flash "fades into the distance" rather than uniformly shrinking.
- **EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE on meteor fall spin** — the weather meteor's 3-rotation spin during its 2s fall was linear. EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE gives the spin a sinusoidal acceleration/deceleration — the meteor starts spinning slowly, speeds up as it falls (gravity accelerating the rotation), then slows as it approaches impact. Reads as physical rotation under gravitational acceleration rather than a constant mechanical spin. Matches the projectile.gd meteor strike spin which already had this easing applied.

### Projectile Meteor Spin + Light Fade Easing (`projectile.gd`)
- **EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE on meteor strike spin** — the Meteor Strike weapon mod's 720° spin during its 0.9s fall was linear. Now matches the weather meteor's spin easing (EASE_IN_OUT + TRANS_SINE) for a consistent "falling rotating object" visual language.
- **EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on Black Hole Launcher + Gravity Mine light pulses** — both the Black Hole Launcher's pull phase light (darkening to -4.0/-6.0 energy) and the Gravity Mine's pull phase light (brightening to 8.0 energy) were linear. EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD gives the light change a gradual start that accelerates — the darkness "deepens" for the black hole and the light "intensifies" for the gravity mine, reading as energy building up rather than a constant-rate dim/brighten.

### Control Rebind Menu UI Fade Easing (`control_rebind_menu.gd`)
- **EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD on entrance fade-in + EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on exit fade-out** — 5 tween_property calls across the entrance (panel, hint label, 2 buttons) and 5 across the exit (title, hint label, 2 buttons, panel) were linear while the sibling tweens (bg, title, panel scale) already had proper easing. Now all UI elements in the rebind menu fade in and out with matching quadratic curves, so the menu appears and disappears as a cohesive unit rather than some elements easing naturally while others linearly fade.

### Wandering Merchant Despawn Alpha Easing (`wandering_merchant.gd`)
- **EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD on despawn alpha fade** — the merchant's 0.6s material alpha fade-out was linear while the sibling scale shrink already had EASE_IN + TRANS_CUBIC. EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD gives the alpha fade a gradual start that accelerates, matching the scale shrink's accelerating collapse — the merchant "fades away faster as they shrink" rather than uniformly dimming while shrinking at a different rate.

### Summary
- **40 linear-default tween_property calls across 16 files** — all non-intentional linear tweens now have proper easing curves (EASE_OUT/IN/IN_OUT + TRANS_QUAD/SINE). The only remaining linear tweens are intentionally linear: turret bolt movement (constant-speed projectile travel) and procedural boss rotation (constant-speed continuous spin). This completes the universal tween easing effort started in Enhancement Pack 42, which added TRANS_QUAD to tweens that had `set_ease()` but no `set_trans()` — this pack catches the tweens that had neither `set_ease()` nor `set_trans()` at all.
## Enhancement Pack 47 — Final Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination

### Void Leviathan Per-Physics-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`enemy_void_leviathan.gd`)
- **`_get_target_player()` now uses `_cached_player` from EnemyBase** — the Void Leviathan's `_get_target_player()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` on every call. This function is called from `_update_boss_attacks()` (line 275: void breath targeting) and `_update_vacuum_pull()` (line 361: vacuum pull targeting), both of which run every `_physics_process` frame. The Void Leviathan is a boss with a long fight (600 HP, 3 stages), so these scans accumulated over the entire encounter duration — potentially hundreds of thousands of scene-tree traversals. Now `_get_target_player()` uses the `_cached_player` field from the `EnemyBase` base class (populated by `_update_ai()` in `super._physics_process()`) with a lazy fallback scan for calls that fire before `super._physics_process()` runs (boss attacks at line 186 fire before `super` at line 194). Matches the caching pattern used across the codebase (`enemy_base.gd`, `collectible.gd`, `wildlife.gd`, `minimap.gd`, `enemy_graviton.gd`, `enemy_spitter.gd`, `enemy_sentinel.gd`, `enemy_ancient_sentinel.gd`, `enemy_gravity_elemental.gd`, `enemy_bomber.gd`, `enemy_drake.gd`, `enemy_wisp.gd`, etc.).

### Pet Training System Per-Frame Group Scan Elimination (`pet_training_system.gd`)
- **`_cached_player` field added** — the pet training system's `_update_dash_course()` and `_update_target_dodge()` each called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` on every frame via `update()` (called from `GameManager._process`). During an active training mini-game (20s dash course, 15s target dodge), these scans ran 60 times per second for the entire duration. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily and only re-scanned when stale (null or freed), matching the caching pattern used across the codebase. The cache is cleared in `reset()` (called on game restart / player death) so stale state doesn't carry into the next run. The fetch frenzy mini-game (`_update_fetch_frenzy()`) doesn't need a player reference (it tracks the pet, not the player) so it was already scan-free.

### Dimension System Rift Spawn Group Scan Elimination (`dimension_system.gd`)
- **`_cached_player` field added** — `_try_spawn_rift()` called `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` every time the rift spawn timer fired (every ~25-45s). While not per-frame, this is called from `_process()` and the scan was uncached. Now a `_cached_player` field is populated lazily and only re-scanned when stale. The cache is cleared in `_on_game_restarted()` so stale state doesn't carry into the next run. The other group scans in `dimension_system.gd` (`_spawn_void_shadow_clone()` at line 361 and `_spawn_exit_collectibles()` at line 420) are event-driven (dimension entry/exit, not timer-driven) and were left as one-shot scans — caching them would add complexity for no measurable benefit since they fire at most once per dimension visit.

### World Boss Manager Spawn Function Cache Reuse (`world_boss_manager.gd`)
- **`_try_spawn_world_boss()` now uses existing `_cached_player`** — the file already had a `_cached_player` field (added in Enhancement Pack 32) used by the despawn distance check in `_process()`, but `_try_spawn_world_boss()` still did its own uncached `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("player")` scan. While this only fires every ~3-5 minutes when the spawn timer hits zero, reusing the existing cache is trivially correct and eliminates the redundant scan. The cache is already cleared in `_on_game_restarted()`.

### Summary
- **4 remaining per-frame/per-tick group scans eliminated across 4 files** — this completes the per-frame group scan elimination effort that spanned Enhancement Packs 29-47. Every `get_first_node_in_group("player")` call that was inside a `_process()` or `_physics_process()` hot path has now been replaced with a cached reference. The only remaining `get_first_node_in_group("player")` calls in the codebase are in event handlers (one-shot callers like `start_game()`, `enter_dungeon()`, `teleport_to()`, `cycle_to_next_slot()`) where caching would add complexity for no measurable benefit, or in files that already have `_cached_player` but use it only for specific functions (the remaining calls are in non-hot paths).

## Enhancement Pack 48 — Dedicated Milestone SFX + Skill Tree Purchase Visual Pulse

### Dedicated SFX for Gameplay Milestones (`audio_manager.gd` + 7 consumer files)
- **4 new dedicated SFX** replace generic sound reuse for 4 distinct gameplay milestone events:
  - **SFX_ACHIEVEMENT** — a triumphant 4-note major arpeggio (C5→E5→G5→C6, 523→659→784→1047 Hz, 0.08s/note, 0.22 vol). The major triad ascending through two octaves conveys "accomplishment unlocked" — the classic fanfare interval. Replaces `SFX_LEVEL_UP` in `achievement_popup.gd` (achievement unlocks) and `leaderboards.gd` (new #1 leaderboard record). Previously the player would hear "level up" when an achievement unlocked — thematically misleading since no level was gained. Now each milestone type has its own sonic identity.
  - **SFX_QUEST_COMPLETE** — a bright 3-note rising chime (G4→C5→E5, 392→523→659 Hz, 0.10s/note, 0.20 vol). The perfect fifth + major third interval conveys "task fulfilled" — a satisfying resolution. Replaces `SFX_COMBO_MILESTONE` in `mission_system.gd` (mission completion), `procedural_quest_system.gd` (procedural quest completion), and `pet_questline.gd` (pet questline quest completion). Previously quest completions sounded like kill streaks — now quests have their own distinct audio identity separate from combat.
  - **SFX_SKILL_UNLOCK** — a crystalline 2-note ascending ping (C6→G6, 1047→1568 Hz, 0.07s/note, 0.18 vol). The high crystalline register conveys "knowledge / skill acquired" — like a rune activating. Short and punchy since skill purchases happen in a menu. Replaces `SFX_COMBO_MILESTONE` in `progression_system.gd` (`purchase_skill()`) and `SFX_LEVEL_UP` in `skill_tree.gd` (`_on_skill_purchased()`). Previously skill purchases sounded either like kill streaks (progression system) or level-ups (skill tree) — now they have a unique crystalline identity.
  - **SFX_VICTORY_FANFARE** — a 6-note triumphant fanfare (C5→E5→G5→C6→E6→G6, 523→659→784→1047→1319→1568 Hz, 0.09s/note, 0.30 vol). The extended 2-octave ascending major arpeggio is the most elaborate SFX in the game — reserved for the ultimate payoff of completing Boss Rush, Speedrun, or Endless modes. Replaces `SFX_LEVEL_UP` in `victory_screen.gd` (`_show_victory()`). Previously the victory screen played the level-up sound — now it plays a dedicated fanfare that conveys "you did it — the run is won" rather than "you gained a level." The milestone wave celebration (`_show_milestone()`) retains `SFX_LEVEL_UP` since milestones are smaller celebrations.
- All 4 SFX added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning (±6%), except `SFX_VICTORY_FANFARE` which is intentionally excluded since the fanfare should always play at the same pitch for consistency — it's a once-per-run celebration, not a frequently-repeated sound.
- Total SFX count: 108 (up from 104).

### Skill Tree Node Purchase Visual Pulse (`skill_tree.gd`)
- **Golden expanding glow ring on skill purchase** — when the player spends a skill point, the purchased skill node now displays a golden expanding glow ring that radiates outward from the node's border and fades over 0.5s, giving the player immediate visual confirmation that their click registered. Previously the skill node's rank bar updated silently — the player had to notice the rank segment filling in to know the purchase went through. The pulse uses the golden color language (1.0, 0.85, 0.3) shared by prestige, achievements, and maxed skills for visual consistency.
- **Inner bright flash** — for the first 30% of the pulse (0.15s), a golden fill overlay flashes inside the node before fading, creating a two-stage "flash → radiate" effect that reads as "energy released on purchase."
- **`_pulse_skill` + `_pulse_timer` state** — tracks which skill was purchased and how long the pulse has been running. The timer ticks down in `_process` and triggers continuous `queue_redraw()` while active so the animation is smooth. State is cleaned up when the timer reaches zero.
- **SFX_SKILL_UNLOCK integration** — the skill tree now plays `SFX_SKILL_UNLOCK` when visible (the progression system also plays it, so the sound fires whether or not the tree panel is open — the tree only plays it when visible to avoid double-triggering).

## Enhancement Pack 49 — Mission System Reset Bug Fix + Remaining Linear Tween Easing

### Mission System State Reset on Game Restart (`mission_system.gd`)
- **Bug fix: missions persisting across runs** — the MissionSystem autoload (registered in `project.godot`) had no `game_restarted` signal handler. When the player died and restarted, `_active_missions`, `_completed_missions`, and `_visited_biomes` were never cleared, so missions from the previous run (including visited biome tracking and completed mission history) persisted into the new run. This meant the player could start a new run with stale objectives, incorrect biome exploration counts, and a full mission board from the previous run instead of fresh missions. Now `_on_game_restarted()` clears all three state collections and calls `_generate_initial_missions()` to create a fresh set of 5 starting missions, matching the behavior of `procedural_quest_system.gd`, `pet_questline.gd`, and all other quest/progression autoloads which already had `game_restarted` handlers. The signal connection is made in `_ready()` alongside the existing GameManager signal connections.

### Remaining Linear-Default Tween Easing (`enemy_base.gd` + `deployable_system.gd` + `projectile.gd`)
- **7 remaining linear-default fade tweens now have proper easing curves** — completing the universal tween easing effort from Enhancement Packs 42 + 46. These 7 tweens were missed by previous passes because they were in weapon-mod on-hit effect functions (not regular gameplay tweens) and had sibling tweens with proper easing that masked the visual issue:
  1. `enemy_base.gd` death fade alpha tween (line 1613) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD, matching the sibling scale tween's EASE_IN + TRANS_CUBIC for a cohesive death dissolve
  2. `deployable_system.gd` shield bubble hit flash onset (line 91) — EASE_OUT + TRANS_QUAD, matching the existing ease on the recovery half of the same flash
  3. `projectile.gd` acid trail pool fade-out (line 1164) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD
  4. `projectile.gd` black hole singularity fade (line 1352) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD
  5. `projectile.gd` magnet mine detonation fade (line 1457) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD
  6. `projectile.gd` black hole launcher collapse fade (line 1572) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD
  7. `projectile.gd` poison nova cloud fade (line 1949) — EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD
  All 7 are alpha fades where EASE_IN + TRANS_QUAD gives a natural gradual-start-then-accelerate dissolve — the effect "fades away faster as it disappears" rather than uniformly dimming. Only remaining linear tweens in the codebase are intentionally linear: turret bolt movement (constant-speed projectile travel), procedural boss rotation (continuous spin), and deployable bolt movement (constant-speed projectile travel).

## Enhancement Pack 50 — Missing game_restarted Signal Handlers on 7 Autoloads

### DungeonGenerator State Reset (`dungeon_generator.gd`)
- **Bug fix: dungeon state persisting across runs** — the DungeonGenerator autoload (registered in `project.godot`) had no `game_restarted` signal handler. When the player died and restarted, `_dungeons`, `_active_dungeon_id`, `_active_root`, and `_entrance_nodes` were never cleared, so dungeons from the previous run's world seed persisted into the new run. The entrance portal nodes (children of the autoload) would remain in the scene referencing the old world's positions, and if the player was inside a dungeon when they died, the `_active_root` interior geometry would leak. Now `_on_game_restarted()` frees the active dungeon interior, frees all entrance portal children, clears all state arrays/dicts, and re-generates dungeons via `call_deferred("_generate_all_dungeons")` so the new run gets fresh dungeons seeded from the new `GameManager.world_seed`. Matches the reset pattern used by `mission_system.gd`, `endgame_manager.gd`, and all other progression autoloads.

### BiomeEffects State Reset (`biome_effects.gd`)
- **Bug fix: biome traps and timers persisting across runs** — the BiomeEffects autoload had no `game_restarted` signal handler. When the player died and restarted, `_active_traps` (Area3D nodes from the Ancient Ruins biome's hidden traps), `_trap_positions`, `_cached_player`, `_heat_tick_timer`, `_wind_timer`, `_wind_direction`, `_trap_damage_timer`, `_current_biome`, and `_player_in_new_biome` were never cleared. Stale traps from the previous run could persist if the player died in an Ancient Ruins biome, and the cached player reference would be freed but still referenced. Now `_on_game_restarted()` calls `_clear_traps()`, nulls the cached player, resets all timers to 0, resets `_current_biome` to GRASS, and clears `_player_in_new_biome` so the system cleanly re-initializes on the first biome change of the new run.

### DialoguePanel State Reset (`dialogue_panel.gd`)
- **Bug fix: active dialogue persisting across restart** — the DialoguePanel autoload (CanvasLayer at layer 90) had no `game_restarted` signal handler. If the player died while talking to an NPC (dialogue active), `_is_active`, `_npc`, `_lines`, `_line_index`, `_displayed_chars`, and the typewriter timers would persist across the restart. The panel could remain visible showing stale dialogue text from the previous run's NPC, and `_npc` would reference a freed node. Now `_on_game_restarted()` force-closes the dialogue immediately (skipping the exit animation since the scene is being torn down), clears all state, and hides the panel.

### TooltipManager State Reset (`tooltip_manager.gd`)
- **Bug fix: tooltip state persisting across restart** — the TooltipManager autoload had no `game_restarted` signal handler. If a tooltip was pending or visible when the player died, `_has_pending`, `_delay_timer`, and `_is_visible` would persist across the restart, potentially showing a stale tooltip for a freed entity on the new run's first frame. Now `_on_game_restarted()` calls `hide_tooltip()`, resets `_delay_timer` to 0, clears `_has_pending`, and sets `_is_visible` to false.

### SpectatorMode State Reset (`spectator_mode.gd`)
- **Bug fix: spectator mode persisting across restart** — the SpectatorMode autoload (PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS) had no `game_restarted` signal handler. If the player was spectating a replay and the game restarted (which can happen via signal from other systems), the spectator camera, HUD, and game-tree pause would persist. The `_spectating` flag, `_spec_camera`, `_spec_hud`, `_spec_hud_canvas`, and `get_tree().paused = true` would all carry over, leaving the game paused with a free-look camera in the new run. Now `_on_game_restarted()` calls `stop_spectating()` if spectating is active, which cleanly tears down the camera + HUD, restores the mouse, and unpauses the game tree.

### ProceduralBossGenerator RNG Re-seed (`procedural_boss_generator.gd`)
- **State reset: RNG re-seeded on restart** — the ProceduralBossGenerator autoload used a `_rng` that was seeded per-boss from `GameManager.world_seed` in `generate_boss_at()`, but had no `game_restarted` handler. While the per-boss seeding was correct, adding a `_on_game_restarted()` handler that re-seeds the RNG from the new `world_seed` ensures the generator's base state is aligned with the new run from the start, matching the pattern used by `dungeon_generator.gd` and `procedural_biome_generator.gd`.

### NavigationManager State Reset (`navigation_manager.gd`)
- **Bug fix: stale nav references persisting across runs** — the NavigationManager autoload had no `_ready()` function and no `game_restarted` signal handler. When the player died and restarted, `_nav_region`, `_is_baked`, `_world_node`, and `_nav_mesh` would reference the previous world's freed nodes. The nav region is added as a child of the world node (freed on scene reload), so the node itself is cleaned up, but the autoload's references to it would be stale — `is_baked()` would return `true` even though the nav mesh no longer existed, and `get_next_position()` could fail silently. Now `_on_game_restarted()` nulls all four references so the system knows it needs to re-bake when `build_nav_region()` is called for the new world.

## Enhancement Pack 51 — Missing Death Particles + UI Transition Polish + Enemy Projectile Impact SFX

### Plasma Serpent Death Particles (`enemy_serpent.gd`)
- **Plasma scatter burst on death** — the Plasma Serpent was the only standard enemy in the game with no thematic death particle burst. Every other enemy type had a bespoke death effect (Crystal Wraith → crystal shatter, Plasma Stalker → plasma burst, Time Warden → temporal burst, Gravity Elemental → gravitic burst, Echo Knight → shadowy burst, Mirror Mimic → mirror-shatter burst, Toxic Spore → toxic burst, Swarm Queen → queen burst, all bosses → boss death spectacle), but the serpent relied solely on the generic base-class death poof. Now `_die()` spawns a 24-particle plasma explosion in the serpent's signature cyan-green `base_color` before scattering segments into mini-enemies, bringing it in line with the rest of the roster.

### Crystal Guardian Death Particles + SFX (`enemy_crystal_guardian.gd`)
- **Crystal shatter particles on death** — the Crystal Guardian's `_die()` had a comment claiming "shatter into crystal fragment particles on death" but the code was just a bare `super._die()` call with no `ParticleEffects` invocation at all — the comment was aspirational dead code. The Crystal Guardian is a crystal-themed enemy that fires crystalline shard projectiles, making the absence of a crystal-shatter death effect a glaring inconsistency. Now `_die()` spawns a 24-particle crystal explosion in `CRYSTAL_GUARDIAN_SHARD_COLOR` plus a `spawn_shield_break_shatter` effect (matching the Crystal Wraith's death visual) and plays `SFX_BREAKABLE` for a satisfying crystalline crack sound, bringing it in line with every other crystal-themed enemy.

### Pet Training Menu Eased Cubic Fade (`pet_training_menu.gd`)
- **Eased cubic fade replaces raw linear alpha** — the pet training menu was the only `_draw()`-based menu in the game that used raw linear `_fade_alpha` for its open/close animation. Every other `_draw()`-based menu (trade menu, equipment menu, quest log, skill tree, fast travel menu) applies `ease_out_cubic` on open (snappy start, gentle settle) and `ease_in_cubic` on close (gradual start, quick finish) for a polished feel. The raw linear fade made the pet training menu feel mechanical by comparison — it started and ended at the same speed, lacking the snappy-then-settle quality of every other panel. Now `_draw()` applies `1.0 - pow(1.0 - _fade_alpha, 3.0)` when opening and `_fade_alpha³` when closing, matching the easing pattern used across all other `_draw()` menus.

### Enemy Projectile Impact SFX (`enemy_projectile.gd`)
- **Impact SFX on player hit** — enemy projectiles had a `SFX_GRAZE` whoosh sound for near-misses but no projectile-specific audio on actual hit. The player only heard the generic `SFX_DAMAGE` from `GameManager.take_damage()`, which fires once even when multiple projectiles hit simultaneously — so a volley of 3-4 bolts connecting at once would produce a single damage sound with no per-hit audio. Now `_on_hit_player()` plays `SFX_ENEMY_HIT` at 0.6 volume before routing damage, giving each projectile impact a distinct audio thunk. The 0.6 volume ensures it doesn't overpower the damage sound or stack into noise during projectile-heavy encounters (Spore Spitters, Crystal Guardians, Drakes, Mirror Mimics).

### Weapon Mod Fusion Menu Show/Hide Animation (`weapon_mod_fusion_menu.gd`)
- **Entrance and exit transition animations** — the weapon mod fusion menu was the only Button-based menu in the game that hard-cut on show/hide (`visible = true/false` with no animation). Every other menu (pause menu, death screen, victory screen, main menu) has a smooth entrance/exit with scale and fade tweens. The fusion menu now scales up from 0.85× with an ease-out-back curve (slight overshoot for a satisfying pop-in) and fades in from transparent over 0.20s on show, and fades out + scales down to 0.92× over 0.18s on hide with a completion callback to set `visible = false`. The bg_panel pivot is set to screen center so the scale originates from the middle. Matches the pause menu's `_animate_pause_in` pattern for visual consistency across all UI panels.

## Enhancement Pack 52 — Statistics Page Completeness + Pet Accessory Menu SFX

### Statistics Page Missing Collectible Names (`statistics_page.gd`)
- **Pet evolution stones added to `_get_collectible_name`** — the `_get_collectible_name()` helper dictionary was missing the 5 Phase 27 pet evolution stones (Ember Stone, Frost Stone, Spark Stone, Void Stone, Leaf Stone, collectible type IDs 12-16). When the Exploration tab displayed the "Items Collected (Lifetime)" breakdown, any evolution stones the player had picked up would show as "Item 12", "Item 13", etc. instead of their proper names. Now all 17 collectible types have display names matching `GameConstants.COLLECTIBLE_NAMES`, so the items breakdown is fully readable.

### Statistics Page Phase 26+ World Life Stats (`statistics_page.gd`)
- **Session tab: 5 new world-life stat lines** — the session tab was missing 5 stats that Statistics already tracks: Wildlife Caught, Treasure Chests, Lore Stones Read, Merchant Trades, and Fast Travels. These are all session-level counters that `Statistics` records via `record_wildlife_caught()`, `record_treasure_chest_opened()`, `record_lore_stone_read()`, `record_merchant_trade()`, and `record_fast_travel()`, but the session tab only showed the original 13 combat/movement stats. Now the session tab shows 18 stats, giving the player a complete picture of their current run's world interactions.
- **Lifetime tab: new "World & Progression" section** — the lifetime tab had a "Best Records" section (Best Score, Best Combo, Best Level, Best Survival Time) but was missing 8 lifetime stats that Statistics tracks: Wildlife Caught, Treasure Chests Opened, Lore Stones Read, Merchant Trades, Fast Travels, World Bosses Defeated, Daily Challenges Completed, Weekly Challenges Completed. These are all lifetime counters persisted to `user://zorp_stats.json`, but the player had no way to see them in-game. Now a dedicated "World & Progression" section below "Best Records" shows all 8, giving the player a full lifetime overview of their world interactions and challenge participation. This is especially important for Daily/Weekly Challenge completion counts, which are tracked by their respective autoloads (`DailyChallengeSystem`, `WeeklyChallengeSystem`) but had no in-game visibility.

### Pet Accessory Menu Hover + Click SFX (`pet_accessory_menu.gd`)
- **Hover SFX** — the pet accessory menu was the only `_draw()`-based menu with clickable elements that had no hover SFX. Every other `_draw()`-based menu with interactive elements (equipment menu, trade menu, skill tree, fast travel menu, pet training menu, replay browser, mode selector) plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` when the cursor enters a new interactive element. The pet accessory menu's `_gui_input` MouseMotion handler updated `_hovered_accessory` and `_hovered_slot` for visual highlighting but never played a sound. Now it plays `SFX_UI_HOVER` when the cursor enters a new craft/equip/unequip button, using `_prev_hovered_accessory`/`_prev_hovered_slot` trackers to fire only on boundary transitions (not every mouse motion frame).
- **Click SFX on craft/equip/unequip** — the pet accessory menu's craft, equip, and unequip button clicks were completely silent. Only the close button had `SFX_UI_CLICK`. Now all three action types play `SFX_UI_CLICK` on click, matching every other `_draw()`-based menu's click feedback. The click sound fires before the action is dispatched so the player hears immediate confirmation.

## Enhancement Pack 53 — Enemy Lunge SFX + Boss Enrage SFX + Weather Shift SFX

### Enemy Melee Lunge Strike SFX (`enemy_base.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_ENEMY_LUNGE** — a sharp descending impact whoosh (500→200 Hz, 0.08s, 0.15 vol) that plays when an enemy executes its melee lunge — the release/strike moment after the windup. Previously the windup had `SFX_ENEMY_WINDUP` (a rising charge tone) but the actual strike was completely silent. The player saw the lunge animation (squash-and-stretch + trail particles) and felt the camera shake + hit-stop on contact, but heard nothing at the lunge moment itself — only the windup charge and the damage sound on the player's end. Now the full attack cycle has a complete audio identity: rising charge (windup SFX) → striking release (lunge SFX) → impact (damage SFX + hit-stop). The descending pitch conveys a committed forward lunge — a predator leaping. Short (0.08s) and quiet (0.15 vol) so swarms don't stack into noise. Pitch scales with enemy size via the cached `_windup_pitch` field (deeper for larger enemies). Volume attenuates with distance via `_compute_dist_atten` so off-screen attacks are subtle. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for natural micro-detuning (±6%). Also fires for mind-controlled enemies attacking other enemies (`_execute_attack_on_enemy`), and the mind-control attack windup (`_try_attack_enemy`) now also plays `SFX_ENEMY_WINDUP` — previously the MC attack path was completely silent for both windup AND lunge, the only attack path in the game with no audio at all.

### Boss Enrage SFX (`enemy_drake.gd` + `enemy_void_leviathan.gd` + `enemy_ancient_sentinel.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_BOSS_ENRAGE** — a colossal deep descending growl (80→40 Hz, 0.45s, 0.40 vol) that plays when a boss enters its enrage phase. Previously all 3 bosses (Drake, Void Leviathan, Ancient Sentinel) reused `SFX_BOSS_SPAWN` (a 60 Hz sustained rumble at 0.6s) at different pitches — the Drake played it at 1.0×, the Void Leviathan at 0.7×, and the Ancient Sentinel at 0.5×. This was a hack that made all three bosses' enrage moments sound like a muffled version of the boss spawn sound. The dedicated SFX_BOSS_ENRAGE starts lower (80 Hz vs 60 Hz), descends steeply to 40 Hz (vs 60→60 sustained), and is shorter (0.45s vs 0.6s) to convey "escalating rage" rather than "ominous entrance." The low 40 Hz terminal frequency gives a visceral chest-rumble quality that distinguishes enrage from spawn. Excluded from pitch variation since enrage is a rare, dramatic event that should sound consistent. All 3 boss enrage functions now call `play_sfx(SFX_BOSS_ENRAGE)` instead of the reused `play_sfx(SFX_BOSS_SPAWN)` / `play_sfx_pitched(SFX_BOSS_SPAWN, 0.7/0.5)`.

### Weather Transition SFX (`weather_system.gd` + `audio_manager.gd`)
- **New SFX_WEATHER_SHIFT** — a gentle atmospheric crossfade sweep (300→500→300 Hz, 0.35s, 0.18 vol) that plays when the weather changes. Previously weather transitions reused `SFX_RIFT` (a dimension-rift whoosh at 0.35 vol) — thematically misleading since the player would hear "dimensional rift opening" when it was just rain clearing or fog rolling in. `SFX_RIFT` is the sound of reality tearing open for a dimension shift, not a weather change. The dedicated SFX_WEATHER_SHIFT is quieter (0.18 vs 0.35 vol) since weather changes are informational, not dramatic. Uses a chime with ascending then descending notes (300→500→300 Hz) to convey "atmosphere shifting" — a gentle breeze rather than a reality tear. Added to `_PITCH_VARIATION_SFX` for subtle per-weather variation so the same whoosh doesn't repeat identically across different weather transitions.

### Total SFX count: 111 (up from 108)
